Knowledge Management and Web 3.0: Introduction to Future and Challenges

Deepmala Singh
Anurag Singh
Sagar Karki

Abstract

Today, it is understood that more data is streaming into organizations from more sources than ever before. But only collecting and storing the data is not enough for every organization, it is important to put them in usable form so that they may get easily accessible whenever they are needed. This overburden of information produced new technologies in many forms. In these new technologies knowledge management and Web 3.0 are also used as a tool for the better use of the data in the productivity of the organization. This chapter will discuss that how integration of new emerging technology, that is, Web 3.0 in knowledge management can impact the working of an organization along with that the chapter also explains the evolution and overview of knowledge management and Web 3.0, the future, challenges, and the significance of the same.

Keywords: Knowledge Management, Knowledge Sharing, Knowledge Stockpiling, Knowledge Transfer, Web 1.0, Web 2.0, Web 3.0,

1 Introduction to knowledge management

With the speedy growth of Internet, knowledge has been increasing rampantly year by year. By 2004 approximately every 18 months the knowledge was doubling (American Society of Training and Documentation (ASTD)), with the advent of Web.

The convenience and approachability of Internet and the popularity of computer have produced a major change in the passion and way of using the information, the popularization of using computer has given a massive transformation from traditional web pages Web 1.0 to a new emerging internet model, which is based on ample content and collaboration known as Web 3.0. Organizing the gathered knowledge on the internet by knowing the language of machines and humans without the help of search engines and the recovery of information are the main features of Web 2.0. Today we are in the era of information society which is not only information based but there are ample opportunities of producing and diffusing new knowledge. Since to achieve success for every enterprise knowledge is a core factor in today’s highly competitive commercial environmental and that is why designing the knowledge management (KM) adjusted with the evolution trend of internet is one of the important topics for all type of enterprises.

Today every enterprise either big or small has been overburdened with information’s by which it has become difficult to separate the useful information from the bulk of given information. The important is to share better and relevant information rather than collecting and sharing more information. Many literatures show that KM 3.0 with the help of Web 3.0 will be a valuable tool in using knowledge rather than gathering and collecting the information [16].

Many researches have been conducted regarding the use of KM and Web 3.0 in various fields, this article aims to discuss how the combination of KM and Web 3.0 influence and give an effective construction of HRM 2.0. This chapter will discuss that how integration of new emerging technology, that is, Web 3.0 in KM can impact the working of an organization.

KM is an interesting issue in business networks. Even though the term “knowledge management” may recommend a somewhat straightforward definition, there are a lot of suppositions on what precisely it is and how it ought to be utilized. Notwithstanding, because of the consistently expanding speed of business advancement, the assignment of successful and serious executives of associations gets fundamental, and KM, whenever comprehended and actualized appropriately, might be a helpful apparatus for business change just as the way to competitive advantage.

Davenport and Prusak [8] see knowledge as a developing blend of outlined insight, values, relevant data, and master understanding that gives a structure to assessing and consolidating new encounters and data. They found that in associations, the information regularly gets implanted in archives or vaults and authoritative schedules, cycles, practices, and standards. They additionally express that with the end goal for information to have esteem, it should incorporate the human increases of setting, experience, and understanding. Nonaka [22] grows this view by expressing that information is tied in with importance in the sense that it is setting explicit. This suggests that clients of knowledge should comprehend and have experience with the unique circumstance, or encompassing conditions and impacts, in which the information is created and utilized with the end goal for it to have significance to them. This likewise infers that all together for an information vault to be helpful, it likewise should store the setting where the knowledge was produced. That knowledge is setting explicit and contends against the thought that knowledge can be applied all around; notwithstanding, it does not contend against the idea of hierarchical information [6]. This part believes hierarchical information to be a vital segment of what authoritative individuals recall and use, implying that knowledge is noteworthy. KM is the cognizant cycle of characterizing, organizing, holding, and sharing the information and experience of representatives inside an organization [5] characterizes information as taking five particular structures: encapsulated, inserted, embrained, encultured, and encoded. He characterizes typified knowledge as knowledge that is acquired through preparing of the body to play out an undertaking [17] characterized KM as the act of specifically applying information from past encounters of dynamic to current and future dynamic exercises with the express motivation behind improving the association’s viability.

With advances in computing, the advent of the internet and more specifically the Web, KM tools have developed. It is no longer just a matter of processing documents, but rather a set of internal and external data so that they can be reused in the future. Here are some examples of tools:

  • Forums or instant messaging: simplifying communication between different players;

  • Word, Excel, videos, etc.: Establishing written or oral procedures;

  • Intranet platforms: sharing news and general documents;

  • Directories: location of different expertise;

  • Working groups: developing a collaborative system and sharing among members of the same organization;

  • E-learning training: optimizing the learning of newcomers and upgrading employees.

Several features or keywords can help identify the KM’s practices:

  • a managerial and/or managerial approach (will, device, measures, etc.) opposed to the idea of letting things happen at random;

  • a goal: to do better (than before or others), to win;

  • a collective approach: moving from the individual who manages his own knowledge to the collective mobilization of individual knowledge to move toward collective knowledge;

  • an inscription in time, in time (memory, sustainability, capitalization, etc.): moving from activism with its short-term “blows” to a thoughtful construction, assured of a future based on the exploitation of the best (and failures) of the past;

  • a vital resource to be mobilized, the knowledge(s), or, in the end, the heads of men, their knowledge, their experiences.

1.1 Significance of knowledge management

KM is significant because it supports the effectiveness of an association’s dynamic capacity. In ensuring that all representatives approach the general expertise held inside the organization, a more brilliant labor force is assembled who are more ready to make brisk, educated choices that advantage the organization. The significance of KM is developing each year. As the commercial center turns out to be perpetually serious, perhaps the most ideal approach to remain on top of things is to construct your organization in a clever, adaptable way. You need to have the option to spot issues in good ways and react rapidly to new data and developments. If for you KM is akin to the vision of a grizzled archivist surrounded by dusty books, then these few lines are for you.

The real challenge is not limited to the acquisition of knowledge, but lies well in his mastery. Because it is essential to identify and manage existing knowledge, to know its veracity, its internal or external criticality, to put in place the best way to collect, sort, disseminate, value it or find it. The KM is undoubtedly a key player in the company. The added value of the company, beyond its capitalization, goes through three fundamental areas necessarily multicultural to respond to an effective globalization:

  • Knowledge of its customers and the market

  • Knowledge of past, current or up-to-date IT technologies

  • The methodologies that enable them to be implemented in industrialization objectives

The KM allows each employee to know and share his knowledge and know-how regardless of his location and his original training. For example, the establishment of virtual skills centers carried by web tools, allows to create a community of men sharing the technical and methodological knowledge of customers through a set of common collaborative tools.

The in-house organization of these skills centers requires synergies between the cross-cutting functions of the company that places the knowledge manager away from the dusty rooms and close to driving change: it requires moving from a way of working based on the power of knowledge to a collaborative way of sharing knowledge.

The goal of the KM is to offer customers increasingly industrialized services while maintaining closeness both to the point of customer knowledge and transparency of the service offered to the customer. Thus, KM is no longer confined to the company but it is a common service that opens a new relationship with the customer, on a common base of information and tools of company content management (ECM). This mastery of collective knowledge allows, beyond the monitoring of the service, an important support to the processes of continuous improvement on the service and transforms the customer/provider relationship into a partnership, bearing the concept of extended enterprise, where end-user satisfaction is at the center. Moreover, the mastery of knowledge and know-how is the guarantee of the mastery of the added value of the company in a context where the modes of communication and information sharing are changing the landscape of IT companies. If our century is indeed that of knowledge, then our industry can only be valued by a constant and visible mastery of its intangible capital. Although this point is now recognized by all, its valuation remains difficult in the markets.

The KM is a major axis of this valuation. Indeed, the mastery of one’s knowledge opens up the fields of knowledge. Knowledge inference and expressing knowledge are the two primary kinds of knowledge covered inside the meaning of KM [3]. Implied information is more instinctive, less simple to bundle and impart to other people. Instances of inferred knowledge are creative reasoning and understanding non-verbal communication. Unequivocal information will be data that is effortlessly arranged and instructed, for example, how to change the toner in a printer and numerical conditions. It will guarantee that the specific information on representatives does not leave with them, or go unutilized by different employees who might profit from that information. It takes into account better situational mindfulness, just as opening entryways for finding out about prescribed procedures, and in general hierarchical improvement.

1.2 Knowledge management: an organizational tool

The need to adjust KM methodology to organization technique was distinguished as basic to the achievement of KM [12, 23]. An organization’s KM technique should make a comprehension of the institution’s KM assets and where they live; articulate the job of knowledge in worth creation, and contain various coordinated undertakings or exercises staged after some time including brisk successes just as long-haul benefits [9]. An institution’s methodology of KM is not discretionary yet depends on the way the organization serves its customers, the financial matters of its business, and the individuals it employs. The question is no longer whether or not, knowledge is a critical organizational resource, but on the alignment of KM to corporate strategy, measurement of KM impact, and driving of business results.

1.3 Knowledge stockpiling

Knowledge stockpiling includes gaining knowledge from individuals from the organization or outer source [15]. Associations are likewise failing to remember while making knowledge and learning. Along these lines, the protection, association, and organization of knowledge that is the organization’s memory establish a significant and successful piece of authoritative information. Aggregate or authoritative memory is characterized as past information, experience, and occasions. Hierarchical memory incorporates information that exists in various structures, including recorded reports, organized data put away in electronic data sets, information on coded individuals put away in master frameworks chronicled authoritative techniques and cycles, and organizations of people and people that learn implied information [14]. Authoritative memory is partitioned into semantic or plots. Semantic memory alludes to general, unequivocal, and expressive information, while plot memory alludes to information in explicit settings and settings. The change of information into semantic memory does not ensure the compelling spread of information, nor does it ensure proficient capacity.

1.4 Knowledge transfer

Knowledge transfer exists at various levels. The exchange of knowledge between people, from individual to group, between groups, across groups, from group to association [4]. Considering the appropriated idea of authoritative agreement, a significant cycle of KM in a hierarchical setting is the exchange of information to where it is required and where it is utilized. In any case, this is not a straightforward cycle since organizations do not have a clue what they know and there is no incredible framework to find and recover information put away in the association. Information transfer comprises five components, to be specific, perceiving the estimation of the information on the source individual, the motivation inclination of the source, the presence and advancement of the correspondence channel, the motivator propensity of getting the individual, and the absorptive limit of the accepting individual [26].

1.5 Knowledge implication

The upper hand of a venture is gotten from the use of information instead of the actual information. It is implied that information itself cannot deliver the upper hand. Simply by utilizing information and applying the part of information can the benefit of the undertaking to be created. The information reconciliation system that makes authoritative capacities is the guidance, association, and free team. The marker alludes to the particular guidelines, principles, methods, and direction shaped by the change of unequivocal information and incorporates information that is adequately spoken with non-specialists through master unspoken knowledge. Authoritative undertakings allude to the type of assignment execution and collaboration, intuitive conventions, and preparing details that permit the application and coordination of individual information without the need to discuss their insight with others. Groups with the essential information and aptitude are joined to take care of issues when task vulnerability and multifaceted nature forestall guidance and hierarchical exchange details.

The challenge of the KM is to be an engine of innovation in two aspects:

  • The financial enhancement of technical innovation with the patent deposit and technical or methodological innovation.

  • The mastery of his knowledge and know-how that allows an inter-technological and methodological sharing more important than before.

One of the foundations of innovation is the use of technologies in other fields than its original domain. The universal science of the enlightenment went toward a specialization that was growing until the end of the last century. The advent of the Internet and a number of sciences brings to light a new vision that carries the silo vision of knowledge back to other times, to propose a heuristic, “web” vision that allows innovative rapprochements that must be followed and supported. Patent filing is the logical conclusion that can only be made with a constant mastery and monitoring of knowledge within the company.

KM is a central player in the company, less greying than it appears. This form of management is at a crossroads between the innovative technophile, the savvy ECM tool user, the ethnologist of the IT market and his own company and the historian of a changing corporate culture at the service of men for the most efficient service to the customer.

2 Knowledge management issues

Increased competition and the management of unforeseen events are pushing organizations to develop their responsiveness. To do this, they must necessarily complement automated systems by the intervention of man and his knowledge. If the direct challenge of KM is to control knowledge within an organization, it results from other strategic issues.

3 Future of knowledge management

KM creates an environment conducive to innovation through the constant improvement of skills. It is the result of careful structuring of the organization and rigorous application of procedures in order to increase the quality and speed of the work.

The time savings, the increase in the skills of the employees, and the transfer of knowledge have many positive impacts:

  • Accelerate the learning capacity of newcomers

  • Promote decision-making by reducing subjectivity

  • Increase team productivity

  • Increase customer satisfaction

  • Adapt more easily to changes (changes in the market, departure of an employee, etc.)

  • Engage and motivate the work teams

Thus, the sharing of expertise at work directly affects the productivity of the company. A productive, responsive, and innovative organization will then be more competitive and more valuable.

4 Introduction to Web 1.0

Technology has improved a lot in many areas of human life and the same improvement has occurred in the internet industry. The news of the launch of the Web 1.0 is not so old since it was first launched in the year 1990, and in 1991, the first-ever website was launched in the world of the internet. Web 1.0 is commonly known as the first interphase in the website world. Web 1.0 was also known as the era of a new information world where people can read and get the information directly by visiting the source. Web 1.0 is also known as a read-only web service, where users can visit the website and read the information without making any interaction or comment for the provided content. In this era, the websites were just simply coded and designed to provide information to a user for reading purposes only, and the users were not able to make any comments or provide reactions if any user wished to do it. Moreover, this system is purely designed in the favor of website owners, since they are the ones who can control the information which they want to provide for their users at any time [13].When the World Wide Web was started, the websites which were created for users were very basic and simple. This stage used to know as the first stage of the website where users can only read static websites. People were not aware from the full content information, and their primary requirement was minimum from websites, which was sufficient for the users. The websites during 1.0 were offering common information-sharing space to users which helps users to understand brochures details, helps them to locate phone numbers, email, fax details, and addresses online at any time for everybody. This system was mainly based on the protocols of XML, HTTP, and HTML programs [25].

5 Web 2.0

The technology was changing frequently in every field, and the same thing has happened in the world of the internet as well. People have now realized that they need more advanced technology and facility in the website world too. It was the time that users were not only convinced for the read-only facility, they were also thinking to update the information if they find any necessary changes. After understanding this basic requirement, Web 2.0 was introduced in the technological world. This second-generation technology in the web system was officially introduced in October 2004 in the internet market. The best feature about Web 2.0 was, in this system, the website had the additional facility of interaction capabilities and it got widely popular as read and write web. There are many popular examples of Web 2.0 websites and each one of us is still using them in our daily life such as different blog websites, social media, and popular sites like Facebook and YouTube [20]. It helps the user to manage and assemble their information-sharing habits and link with the different web applications which were connected with World Wide Web platforms. The biggest example has got the boom in their platforms since more and more people started to use those sites in their regular life. Web 2.0 was the absolute successor of Web 1.0 since it was offering more user-friendly interactive platforms which were useful for the creation of collaborative content, updates, and create flexible web designs. This also helps to introduce new services like chats, playing games, e-learning, blogging, communication, and freedom of tag and comments on anything which are possible to share with everyone. Web 2.0 was more interaction-based with loaded with more web applications unlike Web 1.0, which was only based on information sharing based on static one-way content web forms [1].

6 Web 3.0

Now let us talk Web 3.0, and every individual needs to understand what 3.0 is and what it is capable of. When users search the information on Web 1.0 and Web 2.0, then the system was searching and presenting the information via using the keywords which were searched by the user. However, this outline has changed after the launch of Web 3.0. With the launch of this technology, the machine learning was also included in Web 3.0 which is widely popular as artificial intelligence (AI). Web 3.0 was the semantic web which was combined with read-write-execute the information by the user, where computers and servers can interpret the searched information like humans with AI and machine learning methods. In this system, the algorithm of the programs was specially designed to create communication between machines to the machine. This is the third-generation technology in the field of the World Wide Web which was launched in 2006 by creating this technology with semantic web data integration. Web 3.0 is using the technology with metadata, where it converts the display only data to meaningful information which helps the users to evaluate their searches. The semantic Web 3.0 is the read-write intelligent web feature that is developed by the smart applications to provide users with highly portable and individual-oriented data with next-generation browsing and information searching capabilities while searching for content. This system was executable semantic-based which used the AI and multi-user virtual environment to provide users a better experience when it compared with Web 2.0 which was only based on reading and writing program with online social networks platforms. This feature enables the internet program to help this system to rethink themselves and generate the results rather than humans [7].

After the launching of Web 3.0, it got the hype quickly due to its application services which helps search engines to produce and show more complete, reliable, and targeted information that users want to see in their search. Not even that, it has also helped the people to tightly connect with their personal and professional life and helps them to manage their businesses and friend circle simultaneously. Apart from this, Web 3.0 also helps users to provide a secured maintained record and secured place to store their information digitally so that the user can access it whenever they want. Unlike Web 2.0 where users were only able to connect with the social networks and to share their views, now users can link their feelings, thoughts, and ideas too which is the evolving extension with AI for users in regarding search terms on search engines [2].

Well to be more precise, every technology has its advantages and disadvantages. During my research, I have figured out a couple of advantages and disadvantages of Web 3.0, which will help any user to understand the pros and cons of this technology. Let us start with the advantages first and it starts with increased information linking. This program is based on the semantic-based program where the search engine search feature helps connect data points across the web. Web 3.0 is more efficient when it comes to the matter of web browsing by making communication a breeze while using social network websites. The interaction among people also increased and was spearheaded by faster internet connections, unlike the previous Web 2.0 or Web 1.0 versions. This Web 3.0 makes online interactions easier than offline. Let us move out to the major drawbacks of Web 3.0 and the first one I would like to state is less advanced devices. Web 3.0 has improved a lot of its functions but unless and until if different individuals do not have the devices, they cannot be able to handle Web 3.0 features use those facilities. The complexity increases among users where it can be complicated for newcomers to understand and adapt to the Web 3.0 implementation challenges. The vastness in Web 3.0 needs to eliminate all semantically duplicated terms from billions of pages on the huge internet [21]. The user quarries are not specific and it sometimes can be extremely vague to understand. Web 3.0 also has to deal with scores of uncertain value and deceit the provided data which can lead to logic. To improve all these issues, the semantic blockchain technology could help to interconnecting and storing data in a secure with smart contracts. By using decentralized ledgers in the form of decentralized apps, and using different applications can run on their own without central points of failure in a server farm. It helps track specific information and connects it to other apps responsible for other information. The most interesting part of this development is that nobody knows how long it will take to reach a fully functioning Web 3.0. One thing is sure in Web 3.0, certainly, Web 3.0 will change our online lives if it has improved further and combined with AI to produce and provide more accurate results to its users. The future of Web 3.0 is surely mind-twisting and exciting.

After Web 3.0, it is argued that Web 4.0 is going to launch, however, it will be too early for the individuals to talk about or expect Web 4.0 in the internet market because the idea behind this is still in progress and undergrounded. The biggest thing is there is no significant information or definition discovered yet. However, the major computer science experts believe that the upgraded version of AI is going to be launched soon since many companies are working to enhance their AI and machine learning responses into computer applications. Computer experts also believe that, after upgrading AI in a more precise way, the human and machine can interact and communicate with each other more smoothly to think and make decisions on commands [22].

6.1 The four key properties of Web 3.0

  1. Semantic web – it is the key for Web 3.0 making the data easy to process for machines. It is helping understand the meaning of words to generate, share and connect content through search and analysis. It evolving the user experience to another level of connectivity which leveraging all the available information available online.

  2. AI – AI is one of the major key factors regarding the popularity of Web 3.0. It enables machines to become more intelligent with the vast web data to satisfy the needs of the users. AI allows websites to filter and present the user with the best data possible.

  3. 3D graphics – Web 3.0 has surpassed its predecessors due to its three-dimensional technology where it provides a more realistic three-dimensional online world than 2D. 3D is the base for users’ virtual identities which provides a much better experience from Web 1.0 and Web 2.0

  4. Ubiquitous – the idea of existing or being everywhere at the same time. The rise of mobile devices is increasing day by day and it is easier for many individuals to access the internet anytime anywhere. This internet access makes Web 3.0 more omnipotent and user friendly.

7 Future of Web 3.0

Web 3.0 is blockchain oriented and that means no central point of control, complete data ownership, less interruption of services in permission-less networks. There are myriad reasons regarding the popularity of Web 3.0 in the market and the main reason is their build on a better application system where it will create and produce better-targeted results [10].

8 Conclusion

We now know that knowledge and knowledge represent a fundamental intangible capital for the company. With the advent of the web and it, it is possible to update them regularly. KM is then part of a continuous improvement process. A company’s performance is no longer valued solely in terms of production. It is more its ability to react in the event of a problem, change or competitive development that shows its performance. Workers are no longer seen as mere enforcers. They hold the knowledge and know-how that constitutes the true wealth of an organization. Beside KM Web 3.0 is a term much more used to talk about the future of the Internet than the concrete technical innovations of recent years. At a time when many research sectors offer innovative applications related to the web and the Internet, trying to group them all under one banner seems impossible. If Web 2.0 has finally managed to bring together ideas and identified uses of all, this is not the case with Web 3.0. The technology race of the past 15 years has led to extraordinary innovations, both online and within our software and hardware. A problem then arises: these uses are all linked to the Internet, but not always with the Web.

Impact and Role of Knowledge Management and Web 3.0 in Education Sector: An Empirical Study of University of Kashmir

Afreen Niyaz

Abstract

The process by which knowledge is created, shared, and used is termed as knowledge management (KM) and it is all about changing the way everybody works. The KM is promoted as a significant factor for the organizational sustenance as well as for the upkeep of the worthwhile strength. Intensive discussions have been carried out regarding the importance of KM within our society since past so many years. As we are aware that different technologies have evolved over a period of time, thus reducing the time required for disseminating the knowledge and they are even allowing student autonomy, greater collaboration as well as improved academic efficiency. These applications have mushroomed and at the same time social media has even attracted considerable attention from students of different fields. The popularity of such technologies is influencing the means by which different libraries and other cultural heritage organization’s function. The continuous evolution of the innovative internet has unlocked unimaginable opportunities in web-based education. The present study aims to investigate the usage of Web 2.0 tools and the level of awareness among the Post Graduate (PG) students about Web 3.0 technologies at the University of Kashmir, situated in District Srinagar of Kashmir division. The present study has implemented a descriptive survey method. A self-designed questionnaire was used to gather the required information from 200 respondents. The results obtained showed that the PG students of the Kashmir University are accustomed with most of the tools of Web 2.0 like Instant Messaging, SNS, wikis, and blogs while, they are not acquainted with Podcasts, social bookmarks, or RSS feeds. The study also revealed that the most commonly used tools include WhatsApp, Facebook, YouTube as well as Wikis. The respondents specified interacting with friends and family, sharing ideas for their individual life and connections with new people as the topmost reasons for linking with social networking sites. It was found that the PG students are not fully aware about the latest Web 3.0 technologies and need more guidance in that very field. It is suggested that academic institutions should integrate units related to Web 2.0 as well as Web 3.0 tools into the courses in order to train the professionals of future by improving their proficiencies in order to be competent for the future challenges of using these tools.

Keywords: Internet, Learners, Technology, Knowledge Management, Kashmir University, Social Network,

1 Introduction

In recent times, we have observed the availability of surplus online services among numerous universities and educational institutions which includes procedure for online admissions, virtual (online) learning environments, and online fee payments in order to facilitate and enhance the lifelong learning among variety of students and to make this compatible with other educational management activities as well [5]. Moreover, a teacher now usually creates a purely web-based delivery system which includes online handouts in respect of student’s performance and activities as well as the list of different resources for the reference. We are aware that Web 3.0 is more than just a set of effective and useful new technologies or services. It offers a wide collection of services to make a true online classroom a big real thing. These services have a very positive and real impact on the overall teaching learning process. Such benefits can straight away be aligned to the existing best practices in the online education. Moreover, it is expected that the advantages of this system would be surely aligned to the existing best practices happening in online education and also make further authenticated and resourceful educational environment. The unceasing evolution of information and communication technologies (ICTs) is a doorway to new and innovative opportunities, reforms or improvements in the realm of technology-based education [3]. The current version known as Web 3.0 consists of a more interactive as well as dynamic interface with extremely high advanced features that empower machines to not only think but also to execute the tasks, while providing them with analysis and interpretation skills in order to suit the needs of these users [16]. Hence, it is very important and productive to study and understand the evolution of education technology along with the enhancements that ought to be with Web 3.0 including the way it facilitates teaching and learning process in an advanced manner as well as the limitations that have to be considered and the problems to be addressed.

2 Literature review

The technology has got robust advances, since the development of World Wide Web and all the network of documents has advanced into variety of databases. Such a progress is an outcome of three stages of internet revolution [7]. The phase 1 is called the Web 1.0 which involved a single way communication among the user and the website. Another stage is called as Web 2.0 which started during beginning of 2003 [6]. It offers ability to write or we can say two-way communication is involved [39]. The stage 3 of web, that is, Web 3.0 is a notion of an intellectual web which allows user to not only read but at the same time write and execute web as well [40]. This generation involves a cohesive web experience. The internet indeed has performed a dynamic part in the advancement [14]. It is permitting immense student autonomy, improved pedagogic efficiency, and greater collaboration [26]. These applications have grown so much and thus, social media platform has involved extensive attention from students from diverse streams [13]. Such technologies have now dominated the day-to-day professional as well as personal life of so many users across the world because they are so widespread [12]. Another study revealed that the utmost widespread tool-application areas linked with the web comprise, blogs, vodcasts, social sharing services, folksonomies etc. [19] It has been found that these technologies are so important as libraries can plan very attractive facilities using such applications without there being a need to spend extra budgets for online storage [38].

The third generation simplifies the world-wide data warehouse where from various formats of information are gathered and even shared as well [4]. Web 3.0 also includes three dimensional (3D) components which are its key features [37]. Web 3.0 is referred to as the application where data in a website can be interrelated with the associated information of other websites [10]. Another study suggests that the Web 3.0 is where it is the computer that produces new information instead of humans [46]. It has however been recognized that the machineries can perform tasks with users in the content creation process. Various studies explain the features, applications, and technologies that are linked with Web 3.0. [35]. Moreover, the referenced engines would focus on the specific habits along with the preferences of users [9]. In doing so, it will generate more targeted and complex information which would be of great use [34]. Later this information used on a recommendation engine will be gathered and then stored in a manner which is hierarchical in nature by Intelligent agents [41]. Meanwhile such achievements would give Web 3.0 the capacity to not only gather and analyze but at the same time further distribute the data that can ultimately be transformed into tremendous knowledge, useful information, and immense wisdom [18].

The key elements include:

  • The ability of new programming languages to categorize and manipulate data in order to allow the machines in order to interpret data and also the phrases describing data [2].

  • Information gathering ability from a much wider variety of sources [48].

  • The capability of storing and obtaining contextual information from a web search in a hierarchical manner [20].

  • The ability to generate as well as share all types of data by all types of devices [28].

The uses of both Web 3.0 and Web 2.0 tools are increasingly being adopted at different higher learning institutions globally in order to support the learning practices as well as the facilitation [17]. The launch of this useful semantic web has indeed resulted in a huge turning point about how the digital native might take lead to take advantage from different kinds of personal learning environments, educational software, or even the personal virtual environments [43]. These kinds of environments permit the digital natives the probability of obtaining meaningful data, data filtering and collaboration to suit their specific needs and requirements [36]. A huge number of higher educational institutions are now beginning to understand and interpret the various new techniques and methods of teaching as well as learning, thus leading to the change in educational institution. All this is really needed to fulfil the ever-changing needs of all the citizens of these information-age societies [42].

3 Objectives

  • To identify the level of awareness regarding Web 2.0 tools among PG students of Kashmir University.

  • To recognize the degree of usage of Web 2.0 tools by the PG students of Kashmir University.

  • To find out the level of awareness among the PG students regarding Web 3.0 technologies.

  • To examine the reasons for joining the social media platforms by the PG students.

  • To discuss the role of knowledge management (KM) in the education sector.

4 Methodology

The present study aimed at inspecting the awareness level and usage of Web 2.0 and Web 3.0 tools among PG students of various departments at the University of Kashmir, Srinagar, J&K. The sampling technique used for the study was simple random sampling and the sample size comprised of 200 respondents in number. A self-made questionnaire was designed to gather data from the students studying in different departments of the University of Kashmir. Further, the descriptive statistics were then analyzed with the use of frequency counts as well as percentages. The results that were found were then displayed in the form of tables and diagrams.

5 Results and discussion

5.1 Awareness regarding Web 2.0 tools among students

The present study which involved 200 participants included half of the respondents, that is, (50%) students who were females and 50% being male respondents. The respondents were enquired to show the extent to which they were aware of the tools of Web 2.0 (see Figure 1). Although, students specified that they were not quite acquainted with Web 2.0 tools as their percent was very low which included RSS feeds, podcasts, and social bookmark. The results showed that the PG students of Kashmir University are accustomed with some tools such as Instant messaging, Social networking sites, wikis, and blogs but they are not enough conversant with tools like RSS feeds, social bookmarks, and podcasts. Therefore, the PG students need to be encouraged for using additional applications to gain enough knowledge and remain updated.

5.2 The extent of usage

Respondents were also requested to specify the range to which they were using tools of Web 2.0 (see Figure 2) where it was found that the majority of the respondents (57%) spent maximum time using the social media apps including Facebook, while as 37% respondents were found using Instant Messaging more frequently, 12% respondents were more preoccupied with YouTube platform and used it more often than the other apps for watching videos. In terms of using wikis, 20% of the total participants used it excessively while as 14% spent more time listening to podcasts. RSS feeds and Tagging tools of Web 2.0 were used by a smaller number of respondents and for very less time as they were not quite familiar with these.

5.3 Reasons for making use of the tools

The next question explored the reasons why PG students of the university joined social networks platforms (see Figure 3). To connect with their friends and family, exchanging views for their personal life or meet new people were found to be the top most reasons for linking with the social networks among majority of the respondents. Also, most of them used these to keep themselves updated. It was also found that only a smaller percentage of students were making use of the SNS for sharing knowledge regarding studies and having interest to know new things with 25.5% and 30% respectively. The findings of the study suggested that the PG students do not use the Web 2.0 tools for only restricted to theoretical drives.

5.4 Awareness regarding Web 3.0 among PG students

Respondents were asked if they were aware about the latest web platform termed as Web 3.0 and the response is represented in the Figure 4. Majority of the respondents (65%) were unaware about Web 3.0 applications and did not have any idea of what exactly Web 3.0 refers to and while as 35% of the respondents were fully aware and had enough knowledge regarding Web 3.0 tools and its applications. The PG students having knowledge about Web 3.0 were specifically from the library science background and probably that being the reason they were enough knowledgeable about this new platform of Web 3.0.

5.5 Knowledge of Web 3.0 tools among PG students

Respondents were asked to indicate the level of awareness regarding Web 3.0 tools (see Figure 5). It was found that most of the respondents (28%) had knowledge about the virtual labs followed by 22% respondents who were mindful of online 3D games. Regarding semantic blogs, participants comprising less percentage, that is, 10% were aware about it and 12% had knowledge of virtual 3D encyclopedia. Meagre percentage of participants was found being aware about the rest of the Web 3.0 tools including intelligent search engines, 3D wikis, and semantic forums. There seems to be a huge knowledge gap among PG students regarding these latest Web 3.0 tools and applications. Educational institutions and organizations need to incorporate the teaching and learnings based on these applications so that students can really benefit from them and stay updated.

6 Role of KM in the education sector

An idea that has emerged explosively in different facets of life in the past decades is the KM and the evolution of this KM in the present information era leaped from the understanding that organizational knowledge is a big asset that truly needs to be sustained, nurtured, shared as well as disseminated all across an organization or institutions in order to obtain and also provide solutions to the different organizational problems and concerns [24]. The various techniques and technologies which are specifically used to establish, organize, and build on business expertise and knowledge is known as KM [47]. The concept of KM is related to establishing an explicit knowledge through an organizational and organized process for attaining and governing the flow of knowledge of the employees [32] and this is indeed to make sure that organizations or institutions must achieve their targeted mission as well as vision statement. KM has three important components which include: people, process, and technology. The main instruments of every society are the educational organization for the constant pursuit of knowledge and the essential part of KM in the educational institutions is very important. In other words, we can say the main function of educational organization is the KM [11]. Moreover, through the application of organized thinking, KM in educational organization can surely be uniquely identified as well as studied at various administrative, teaching and learning, research process, student service and lastly the human resource subsystems [21]. The use of the such systems and principles in various educational institutions can develop more flexibility into decision-making processes, easy access to scientific resource, promotion in teaching and learning processes, establishment of the effective internal and external communication network, synergy at the students and faculty knowledge and improvement in quality as well as amidst the research activities in different educational institutions [25]. An important and intrinsic feature of KM is the creation and the development of knowledge in an effective way [30, 8, 33] and this creation of knowledge is very essential for the survival and maintenance of any organization or institutions especially the educational ones. We are aware that knowledge creation is an activity which occurs throughout daily activities, either at work or at the social settings. However, it occurs in different form that could be through humanistic means like talking with people or other formal trainings or even through the technical mechanisms, for example, data mining [22]. It is essentially a human process perhaps technology can facilitate creation of knowledge but it cannot replace people [23]. Mostly organizations have a greater influence over their ability to innovate, create knowledge, and generate value with the new knowledge and it is this knowledge which leads to innovative ways of personal and professional improvements including strategic decision-making capabilities [27]. Moreover, it is very essential for any organization to have the competency to create knowledge as well as generate a competitive advantage that wishes to remain sustainable [15].

The reasons for use of KM are as follows:

  • For the productivity of organization

  • To promote teamwork as well as collaboration

  • To encourage and improve knowledge sharing

  • For effective as well as timely services in management

  • To develop and boost innovation

  • To guard the important knowledge from being vanish

  • To encourage outstanding positive work environment

  • To endorse critical thinking among employees

  • To boost competitiveness in the organization

Indeed, the organizations incorporating KM would surely flourish and achieve its targeted goals in a very effective and efficient manner. It would prove beneficial for not only the organization but for the community as a whole [44].

6.1 Functions and influence of KM activities in educational systems

Research activities
(technical subsystem)

  • Facilitate interdisciplinary research.

  • Increase accountability and competitiveness of researches.

  • Reduce research time and cost.

  • Improve the quantity and quality of the studies through linking researchers into electronic resources, databases, databanks, etc.

  • Facilitate the implementation of research based on cross-cultural dynamism in universities as well as to help in the collection of data and information gathering etc.
Educational planning and curriculum development

  • Improve and update educational planning rapidly.

  • Improve students’ accountability and faculty by making use of past experiences.

  • Encourage quality of different educational programs by applying best practices and also monitoring outputs.

  • Expand administrative services related to educational processes.
Administrative services (administrative subsystem)

  • Support management processes.

  • Improve facilities for the students and faculties.

  • Improve services to internal as well as external stakeholders.

  • Enhance capabilities in identifying activities.

  • Attempt to remove the centralization in providing services and connect all sections into knowledge resources.

  • Increase efficiency and effectiveness of services provided.
HRM
(human resource subsystem)

  • Strengthen communication that is informal in nature so as to achieve set university goals.

  • Prepare as well as update human resources.

  • Establish a reasonable system of salary and bonus crosswise the organization.

  • Facilitate responsiveness capabilities and effective communications.
Students activities

  • Create a context of social growth for students from various cultures and communities by providing cyber climate for their discourses.

  • Improve cooperative activities among students by providing the socio-technological factors for sharing of experiences and knowledge.

  • Generate an extremely flexible environment for the overlapped learning.
Structure subsystem

  • Lead to integration of KM concepts in the academic sphere to enhance the efficacy of external alliance partnerships and to increase the output of organizational processes.

  • Preserve organizational resources by improving the knowledge within the organization and utilizing that knowledge for teaching and learning purpose.

  • Combine both explicit and tacit knowledge shared by staff and faculty through KM.

7 Conclusion

It is concluded with the remark that this study provides information on PG students’ familiarity and their usage of tools related to Web 2.0. The study revealed that the PG students of University of Kashmir are accustomed with Web 2.0 tools such as SNS, blogs, wikis, and Instant Messaging. On the other hand, the results also revealed less awareness about the Web 3.0 tools and applications among the PG students of University of Kashmir. Web-based collaborative learning is the most promising learning model in this era of digital learning and moreover the advent of these latest technologies has provided many innovative ways for efficiently building learning groups. Now these groups can easily enable learners to discuss, share, and give feedback to others through the mode of online medium. Also, it permits learners to develop not only their problem-solving but at the same time their critical thinking skills as well. The present paper also discussed about the role of management of knowledge in higher education. It surely benefits the educational institutes in order to enhance their capacity of information gathering and also apply these to problem solving techniques and even encourage the research and frequent upgrading of their work. KM of the educational system must reflect and encompass information at all levels starting from the level of management to the student level in order to develop professional knowledge of the employees. Since it manages large data thoroughly, therefore it can prove to be an extremely powerful tool to augment productivity and decrease cost in the gathering of an enormous volume of data. Moreover, it is very problematic to record tacit knowledge created by any institutional staff. Therefore, if KM practice is being functioned in all institutions as a constant activity only then the knowledge generated could be apprehended as well as preserved for the future use.

Knowledge Management Vis-à-Vis Adoption of Mobile Health Record Application for COVID Pandemic

Vineet Chouhan
Shubham Goswami
Pranav Saraswat

Abstract

The use of electronic records has been widely accepted as an effective means of enhancing healthcare delivery. Despite advances in medical technology, healthcare continues to present worldwide difficulties of affordability, complexity, and access [1, 2]. Healthcare companies have issues regarding processes and information incorporation. Information is fragmented and often contradictory. Knowledge management can allow healthcare providers to access and exchange information about patients in present COVID situation. Healthcare systems are increasingly adapting to growing clinical demands that can be enhanced with the improvement of knowledge of the patients and using knowledge-based technology. Knowledge-based technologies can successfully help pandemic agencies by encouraging universal information sharing and rapid real-time monitoring. Acceptance of consumers is one of the core elements in the growth of information management systems (IMS). Research suggests a model of the variables that affect the contribution of KMS repositories. The research utilizes the unified principle of adoption and usage of technology (UTAUT) to define variables that may have an effect on the decision to use mobile health records (mHRA) to make a contribution to information.

Keywords: Mobile-Based Health Record Application (mHRA), Knowledge Management, COVID Pandemic,

1 Introduction

Over the last decades, healthcare sectors have experienced tremendous changes in most of the countries in the world due to a rapid advancement in information and communication technology (ICT). Despite advances in medical technology, healthcare continues to present worldwide difficulties of affordability, complexity, and access [1, 2]. Healthcare companies have issues regarding processes and information incorporation. Information is fragmented and often contradictory. E-health needs improved knowledge management systems (KMS) to provide access to and interpret enterprise-wide knowledge for market intelligence. In comparison, information is not utilized for a competitive benefit, since the consistency of consumer choices is directly linked to the quality and availability of knowledge. These multiple knowledge bases cause inedibility incompatibility of format and limited ease of usage. Moreover, number of KMS suffers from non or under use mainly because of a lack of contributions to the system. That is why an understanding of the drivers for adoption of KMS is a priority in knowledge management (KM) research.

In healthcare, KM is being developed mainly in the domain of electronic health record management and the amount of knowledge processing requirement in today’s medicine is huge. In present pandemic time, IT tools that facilitate the capture and distribution of clinical information are becoming vital. Mobile application can support the creation; storage; retrieval; transfer and application of knowledge include data management and learning tools. These KM technologies are important components of KMS and have become central to healthcare [3, 4]. The amount of knowledge, created by all of healthcare partners, is tremendous. Information produced by one partner is of the utmost importance to all others for the delivery of quality treatment. Latest research shows that mHealth (mobile health) may be a blessing brought on by ICT and is undoubtedly one of the most popular programs with significant impacts on the growth of KM in the healthcare field.

In this respect, mHealth technology promotes KM and also has the ability to enhance public health systems in developing economies like India. Acceptance of mobile devices in the health record may create awareness as a platform that signals quality health services and gives the public health service a good image [5]. Technology allows the creation and capture of information. The architecture promotes the codification of information and the diffusion of knowledge to standardize the level of patient care. The primary goal for EHR systems and related technologies should be to facilitate KM for improving individual and community health. Moreover, it has been argued that EHR system is a must have tool for public health officials to improve treatment, care, and prevention COVID and its diagnosis.

Information management can help mitigate medical mistakes and thus their costs by giving decision-making assistance to clinicians. Healthcare is a dynamic area, and collaboration between various providers of healthcare is essential for the delivery of quality care. Hence, cooperative diagnosis can be achieved by the healthcare actors via the implementation of KMS. Past studies showed strong evidence that KM implementation in healthcare is encouraging, that can impact decision-making. It can be said that implementing KM in healthcare, such as advances in healthcare communication technology, mobile technology can be the way forward to improve the quality of care for patients, which is the ultimate goal of healthcare [3, 4].

2 Review of literature

Software and gadgets allow us to log in our operation, provide guidance and boost our efficiency in certain instances [6]. Free wellness is an affordable and flexible way for us to be vigilant at self-managing our well-being, from app push reminders to wearables that provide sensory feedback [7]. Device abundance in wellbeing is now available in devices and wearables to better tackle safety, wellness and numerous therapies issues. These Even, they give updates and nudges to hold us on board. The usage of different technology technologies and mHealth apps has often addressed several other (high) risk behaviors. In India, although several applications focused on the smartphone are being created. The national health portal identified 74 apps that can be used for health information. Still, newly created ‘My Health Record’ and ‘Aarogya Setu’ apps are being established by the Ministry of Health during and after COVID. It may allow for the early diagnosis of numerous medical disorders, including certain types of diseases that can with COVID, harm the human lives. When the proliferation of smartphones and portable apps grows, their usage further encourages a more patient-centric attitude. Advanced digital health infrastructure access is transforming medical treatment by providing new approaches to track and treat illnesses and make care more useful and comprehensive [8].

mHealth application is seen by those in need of healthcare like India as being a simple and inexpensive way to improve their access to health services. The increasing smartphone user in the county also provides an opportunity to develop a mobile-based health record system to improve healthcare. It is also evident from the past studies that mHRA have capabilities transforms healthcare from simple monitoring self-healthcare to supervised and assisted healthcare services. Despite the possible advantages of mHRA systems, recent findings suggest that approving such proposals appears to be a challenge. Ahmed et al. [9] said that many people, while few have used mHRA, were conscious of and considered to be potentially useful. Just 5% of participants in the study used mobile facilities on their phones, and 73% of respondents knew about the potential but were not able to do so. The research Yi et al. [10] has found influences that affect mHealth services acceptance such as perceived ease of use, program consistency, subjective normativity, perceived value, device performance, and technical anxietary. In Bangladesh, Akter et al. [11] have studied the standard of service mHealth adoption. It was found to affect mHealth systems both directly and indirectly. All the above studies examine mHRA acceptance but provided diverse outcomes regarding adoption. Such studies indicate however clearly that most individuals in India are aware of this technology and are involved in it. Despite the potential advantages and the various mHealth initiatives in place, recent studies show that the adoption of this service remains insignificant.

The report on the implementation and usage of mHRA to support KMS from end-user perspective has been done little work. To date, most studies have continued to concentrate on lifestyle, user experience, and security acceptance of mHRA [3, 12, 13, 14], resistance and technology anxiety, and attitudes. Moreover, prior readings have primarily engrossed on medical specialized views for implementation [15]. Few studies have focused on the end-user approval of Health application to contribute to KMS. However, this study primarily emphasizes on identifying barriers in adopting mHRA by using a human-centric perspective and applying socio-technical characteristics of facilitating condition and social influence, which have not been spoke about the implementation of mHRA facilities.

As limited studies have been conducted on health information system implementation in emerging republics, makes the present study outline more relevant and necessary. With respect given the fiction review, the present research attempt to fill above gaps by examining the recognition issues of mHRA in a developing country like India Acceptance by consumers is one of the main elements for the advancement of information management systems. The research suggests a model of the variables that affect the contribution of KMS repositories. Specifically, in the present study, a UTAUT-established prototypical is cast-off to consider the behavioral purpose of end-users in India to agree to take and use mHRA for knowledge contribution.

3 Theoretical framework

The Unified Theory of Acceptance and Use of Technology (UTAUT) is used, as the speculative outline to empirically test the factors that impact the adoption of mHRA developing country context [16]. The UTAUT is the most popular user approved and behaviors analysis model, as it is derived from theory of planned behavior (TPB); theory of reasoned action (TRA); technology acceptance model (TAM); innovation diffusion theory (IDT); PC utilization model (PUM), and the social cognitive theory (SCT). The model is aimed to attain a unified opinion of consumer recognition, is and appealed to predict 70% of the modification in purpose to use new technologies [17].

Using a review of prior studies of UTAUT in healthcare [18, 19, 20], the most used structures in this analysis are the latest science. The computational analysis model includes the systems commitment expectativeness (EE), Performance Expectancy (PE), Facilitating Condition (FC), social impact (SI), and use behavior (UB). As UTAUT is a most robust framework for predicting technology acceptance with good predictability. However, in particular in developed nations, its sufficiency in forecasting technological acceptance in healthcare is uncertain [21]. Some authors used UTAUT variables with theoretical models for determining antecedents in healthcare [22, 23, 24]. Because mHRA adoption is highly context-specific [25], it is important to examine the opinions of machine users.

In order to increase predictability of UTAUT in a healthcare environment the addition of domain-specific determinants can also be considered [26, 27]. Technology adoption in developing countries like India is influenced by background specific societal values and confidence concerns related to obstacles, such as the digital divide. In addition, in order to increase its predictive capacity, context-specific variables are applied to the simple UTAUT model. An updated UTAUT is then used to suit further into the study context. Constructs of self-efficiency and trust dimensions have been added to the model. This is due to the perceived lack of experience and its associated trust issues related to data privacy issues with a mobile application in the Indian context. The following section explains each construct with hypotheses developed to investigate the relationships. It is defined as a degree to which an individual believes that using the system will help to gain healthcare experience.

3.1 Performance expectancy (PE)

Venkatesh et al. [16, 17, 21] have found that PE is the strongest determinant of a user’s behavioral intention (BI) and was identified as convenience expectancy, outcome expectancy, and time expectancy. Studies by Pai and Huang [28], Sun et al. [29] and Carlsson [30] also confirmed the significant positive influence of PE on the intention to use mHealth services.

The above discussion led this study to posit the following hypothesis:

H1. PE has a positive impact on the intention to use mHRA

3.2 Effort expectancy (EE)

It is defined as the ease with which the mHRA is used. Past experiments have shown that EE has a major effect on the desire of consumers to use mHRA [29, 30]. The use of mHealth tracking devices through smartphones has been described as a major motivator [31]. Therefore, it is assumed:

H2. EE has a positive impact on the intention to use mHRA

3.3 Social influence (SI)

The extent to which a person understands the value of peers’ viewpoints and can use mobile medical record applications is defined. Work has shown that SI significantly influences the desire of consumers to use technology [29, 31]. Wills et al. [31, 32] have reported that the behavioral aim for mobile digital wellbeing is an important association between the IS and consumers. The following theory was raised in line with the aforementioned discussion:

H3. SI has a positive impact on the intention to use mHRA

3.4 Self-efficiency (SE)

It is the belief of performing a particular behavior. Self-efficacy perceptions have been found to influence decisions about what actions to undertake [34]. Educational researchers have employed self-efficacy instruments in various disciplines and academic settings [35]. Studies also measure self-efficacy specific to the online environment [34, 36, 37]. Cho and Castañeda [38] studied personal dedication with a smartphone application to learn Spanish. Few types of research also study found the Motivational and emotional commitment to learning Spanish with a mobile application. Few studies also found self-efficacy positive impact on the intention to adopt mHealth apps [39, 40]. The following theory was raised according to the discussion:

H4. SE has a positive impact on the intention to use mHRA

3.5 Trust (TR)

Views on security and confidentiality have received interest in recent literature on online transactions. The perception of risk to safety and threat of personal data loss may differ with a change in demographic of consumer groups. This negative emotional response, the intention of adoption is diminished by anxiety or frustration with technology. Older adults are also reported to have significant problems of confidence [41]. This analysis thus expands the fundamental UTAUT with an additional confidence variable to increase the prediction power. The following theories have also been suggested:

H5. TR harms the intention to use mHRA

3.6 Facilitating conditions (FC)

It can be described as to what degree a person assumes that the usage of mHRA is enabled by an organizational and technological infrastructure. Conditions of facilitation require service scale products, protection and secrecy, technical difficulties. The direct effect of FC technology help on the behavioral purpose and technical use is supported by previous studies [10, 42]. The Boontarig et al. [31] research has demonstrated that FC has had a positive effect on the behavior. The argument above lead to these hypotheses:

H6. FC has a positive impact on the intention to use mHRA

H7. FC has a positive impact on the actual use of mHRA

In the literature on technical implementation, the relation between behavioral Intention (BI) and actual action (UB) is well established and notes that BI is a reliable predictor for actual use. [17, 21, 41]. The present study also tests this relation, and the following hypotheses were proposed:

H8. The BI has a positive impact on the actual use of mHRA

Individual stronger orientation toward knowledge is more associated with the notion of usage. We accept the positive relationship existing between intention to use mHRA systems and knowledge sharing behavior. To test this relation, the following hypothesis was proposed:

H9. The usage behavior has a positive impact on perception toward contribution to KMS Figure 1 present the Research Model and Table 1 presents the summary of construst with scale items as under:

Table 1:Summary of constructs with scale items.

Constructs Scale items

KM3. I like to contribute my knowledge for development of integrated KMS.

4 Methods

All the predictors for the modified model (EE, PE, SI, FC, SE, TR, BI and Use Behaviour (UB)) are measured using scale items adapted from similar studies in healthcare.

In the first section, population information is included in the questionnaire used in the survey and in part B, questioning for the various buildings mentioned in the sample sequence. The pilot study of 25 respondents was performed to test the suitability of the questionnaire. The pilot analysis is used to render contextual changes.

The census of research consisted of smartphone users in select major cities of India, representing the urban population of the country. Due to their age group (≥60), their technological skills and their mobile / internet use experience they were chosen. Responses from survey items measuring theoretical constructs captured via a 5-point Likert scale with reactions ranging from “strongly disagree” (1) to “strongly agree” (5).

5 Result

The research circulated 3,000 questionnaires online and 2,140 of them were analyzed. In terms of demographic profiles, 56% of males and 44% of people who participated in the study belong to various stages of age. The majority of respondents belonged to the service sector (55%) and aged between 30 and 50 years. In terms of access, the majority of respondents owned a smartphone, with 65% of them are using it for five years. A total of 58% of respondents have experiences in using mHRA, 42% are not using but willing to adopt the mobile smartphone-based application. The respondents profile is presented in table 2.

The first phase of analysis, the validity of the measurement model is tested. Item Loadings of all the factors are higher than the threshold value of 0.5. This implies that these variables converge on a Latent Variable common point. It proves the integrity of the house. Composite reliability (CR) of all the observed variables is higher than 0.7 indicate excellent safety of the factor structure. The internal reliability was evaluated considering Cronbach’s alpha. The Alpha Cronbach values of the factors exceeded 0.7, so that the variable can be accurately evaluated further. In order to assess the discriminating validity of each house, the average vector extraction (AVE). The model fits indices used for measuring the data’s performance (Table 3).

In order to analyze the relationship between the structures in the research model, the structural model was established. The path coefficients were also determined to assess the effect of each element on the deciding variable (as per Table 4) at this second step of research. The path coefficients (parameter estimates) were used to assess the magnitude and direction of relationships and also to test the research hypotheses. The structural model was estimated by AMOS 22 to represents relationships among observed and unobserved variables using path diagrams. The greater value of R2 revealed better model quality. The results revealed that all the hypotheses are significant. The results show that the relationships between PE and BI (B = 0.397, p < 0.05); EE and BI (B = 0.252, p < 0.05); SI and BI (B = 0.235, p < 0.05); SE and BI (B = 0.412, p < 0.05); FC and BI (B = 0.213, p < 0.05); FC and UB (B = 0.374, p < 0.05); and BI and UB (B = 0.381, p < 0.05), and UB and KMS (B = 0.256, p < 0.05) is positive and significant. Furthermore, the path coefficient between TR and BI (B = −0.078, p > 0.05) is not significant. Therefore, the majority of hypotheses formulated to test the relationship are significant.
