6 Conclusion and discussion

The research analyzed causal factors for use and implementation of mHRA by evaluating a UTAUT model. End-user acceptance is essential in order for any smartphone application to be successful. Given the strong context-specific use and usage of these programs, faith and auto-efficiency variables are also used in the study of acceptance. The review of the literature shows that few acceptance trials in developed countries have been carried out, in particular using UTAUT. In developed countries, there is little information about UTAUT’s adequacy in mHealth adoption. This thesis was based on a sound technological recognition paradigm and testing methods. The findings of our research clearly indicate that patients should provide patient reports that they find important for better safety and healthcare quality.

The sample was comprised exclusively from smartphone users across major urban locations in India. This research uses the UTAUT model to determine the users’ BI to use Mobile-based health applications. The findings have given a snapshot into UTAUT buildings such as PE, EE, SI, SE, TR, and FC which affect the adoption. The research confirms the suggested conclusions on buildings in operation. Furthermore, the behavioral response was measured using a multidimensional method. The findings indicate the import variables that are helpful to boost their efficiency in implementing mHRA. For a productivity effect, the factors analyzed are critical. Seven of our eight theories for study have been endorsed with high statistical significance.

The findings of this work are consistent with previous work and BI studies in embracing and using mHealth. The findings of previous research in the use of the UTAUT implementation model in e-health applications are consistent. PE and EE have major impacts on the application of health information systems (HIS) according to Phichitchaisopa and Naenna [43]. The main effects of PE and EE on adoption of electro-medical documentation (EMR) have been confirmed by Hennington et al [44]. Holtz and Krein [45] also found a positive relation between PE and SI in the adoption of EMR systems. Wills et al. [33] indicated that SI is more significant in an environment where the user has inadequate experience of online services. In India, cell phones are also a new phenomenon for others than telephony calls, such as health advice.

Such findings suggest that the architecture of EE allows to embrace and use a mHRA. It also says that an easy-to-use interface is important. To use mHRA, it should be efficient and user-friendly in conjunction with the desired workloads. The findings of our analysis are similar to the findings from UTAUT [16] with less direct effect. It can be explained with the low maturity of mobile applications in India. FC like training, online technical support, and IT infrastructure needed to accommodate such systems in developing countries. As a consequence, it is possible that such programs will fail until the program has fulfilled the infrastructural needs. Social effect (SI) relies heavily on people around the consumer in adoption. It can be concluded that most of them were found more likely to install the application, if it became mandatory and if other in the group is using it.

In a multidimensional system the existence of a heavy influence by SE and EE was obtained. In this context, self-esteem, and the desire to use the program seem to play an important role as a way to affect the success of improved use of the mHRA. The construction of SE often leads greatly to the approval of the enterprise. The paper better understand the adoption of KMS with the help of the application of a model, UTAUT, adapted to the specific context of KM. Findings thus validate the use of the UTAUT model in the specific context of KMS. Using an empirical study, researcher validated a part of UTAUT, relating to intentions to contribute to KMS.

7 Implications and limitations of the study

Measurement of the behaviors and the intention of consumers to use the program when the purpose is to seek an explanation of the actual use of the system. The results show that to increase the use of mHRA to support clinical practice; it is essential to focus on the usefulness, user-friendliness to improve the SE of the application – most of the studies in a healthcare measure the effectiveness of the systems. But with the current research on mHRA acceptance, we focus on the actual behavior of end-users. Results are useful for decision makers, healthcare administrators and clinicians in developing economies. The findings of this research add greatly to literature potentially of healthcare application. KM insights are given for the deployment of mHealth technologies for frontline health professionals in developing economies. The research used frequently applied technology acceptance model for studying user acceptance of the technology. Still, only a few studies have investigated technology acceptance from an end-user perspective for more applications. For a developed world or in emerging countries with little focus on implementing MHRA, most recent studies follow the UTAUT pattern.

KM can allow healthcare providers to access and exchange information about patients in present COVID situation. Healthcare systems are increasingly adapting to growing clinical demands that can be enhanced with the improvement of knowledge of the patients and using knowledge-based technology. Knowledge-based technologies can successfully help pandemic agencies by encouraging universal information sharing and rapid real-time monitoring. The study showed that the behavioral intent to use the mHRA has considerable effect on success anticipation, commitment expectations, social control and productive self-management, and also findings indicate that the usage of the software does not have a substantial association between the use of the confidence and the consumer behavior. Findings provide service providers and decision makers with useful knowledge in designing a plan to incorporate this technology to motivate users for knowledge creation and sharing. In this research, policy makers and decision makers plan to introduce mHealth apps in this country also make significant contributions in developing a comprehensive KM framework. The Indian government is setting the basis to carry out the national health plan. The variation in outcomes is based on the study context and the dependent variables chosen and previous experiments are based on them. In this analysis, end-user opinions and the purpose to use the program are known. That would make the decision-making process more educated and improve the potential for contribution towards knowledge creation, storage and sharing.

Empirical studies provide realistic advice in developed countries like India on a successful mHRA adoption and knowledge sharing attitude. For an expanded consumer awareness Mobile-based Health record application services, developers and providers of application can understand the challenges regarding the design of mHRA. This study also gives importance that in order to enhance UTAUT’s explanatory mode power in the context of developing countries, researchers should use context-specific dimensions to modify it. The survey can provide developers with a framework in which BI and real use focusing on age demographics can be influenced. This study could be modified easily to help other developing countries implement similar health-based applications due to a generic approach.

Another result deserves particular attention, which is the link between usage behavior and contribution to KMS. The theoretical hypothesis supports a relationship between the two variables. This revealed the fact that experienced individuals integrate the sharing of knowledge and can develop positive attitude for contributing toward KMS. Implementation of KM in healthcare can provide opportunities in providing the right knowledge at the right time, i.e., at the point of decision making by implementing KM in healthcare is paramount. Appropriate tool like mobile-based systems and user-friendly interface is a requirement as it can significantly improve the quality and safety of care provided for patients at both hospital and home settings. At present evidence-based medical practice is critical for developing countries. However, this can be applied successfully if and only if the right system is in place for KM, and thus, it should be given due attention. It is important to allow use of the available developments in mobile devices and decision support systems, electronic health record systems.

Future research should concern the importance of testing a complete model that covers cognitive, affective, and behavioral responses. Limitation of the study includes reduced sample size belong to only urban cities in the country. Thus, it also raises concern about the generalizability of the findings. Future studies can expand geographic coverage and include more demographic variables in studies. The responses also suffer from the bounded rationality since the users may be in the early stages of adoption of mobile-based health record system. Further, the actual usage is measured with self-reported actual behavior, which is not a direct measure that can be generated by system logs. Another extension of the present study could adopt both quantitative and qualitative data analysis approaches to uncover users’ in-depth opinions on acceptance and usage issues.

Diversified Landscape of Knowledge Management: Transformation in View of Organizational Sustainability

Navita Mahajan
Seema Garg
Oridoye Tomide Hakeem

Learning objectives

  • Knowledge and knowledge-based view of firms

  • Insight into knowledge management with diversified landscape for organizational sustainability

  • Knowledge transformation and web transformation in view of Web 1.0 to Web 3.0

Abstract: The changing landscape of modern society, diversified aspects of global economy, knowledge-based models, and multidimensional theories of firms require global perspectives of innovative thoughts. Organizations are to be prepared for the future and scrutinized that what these businesses are doing best from the rest. The competitive environment of firms is changing swiftly, hence knowledge-based management and its transformational practices are crucial for sustainability and self-reliance of any businesses. The time has come for traditional sources of knowledge to get new paradigms, technological changes, highly advanced modern practices, intangible characters, and pertinent information, all gaining priority in a firm for long-term sustainability. The advanced platforms such as Web 3.0 processes for increased efficiencies are creating incentives for innovation, resulting in entrepreneurs seeking opportunities in changing dynamics and then adapting to these transformations.

To get more insight into the nature and content of knowledge, this chapter traces the transformational basis of knowledge management in terms of the emergence of the knowledge-based view of the firm, its changing dimensions, and a multifaceted review of received literature in which author has examined the knowledge models concepts through multiple subject matter lenses. Knowledge management has provided an umbrella for a range of management activities – all linked together by their central focus on knowledge within the organization. By focusing on some of the core elements of knowledge led theories of firm, changing aspects of knowledge management system, it has been found that those factors have been highlighted which influence firm’s performance, ability of affirms to construct and design the landscape for effectiveness.

The chapter has covered knowledge-based view of the firms wherein the theoretical approaches from economies and organization theory in changing scenario have been discussed by focusing on theory of firm. Later on the knowledge variations in terms of technology, innovation, science, acquaintance description, explicit, tacit interfaces along with paradigms of constructing scientific knowledge are depicted. Later on, the study links knowledge innovations’ influence in view of new economic and social environment with advancement in Web 3.0 technology for competitiveness of organizations. The chapter analyzes different approaches to share, develop, and formalize knowledge for sustainable new approaches in an organization.

Keywords: Knowledge, Landscape, Models, Sustainability, KM, Web, Innovation,

1 Introduction

Knowledge is the prime and supreme for most of the firm’s competitive advantage. This includes both codified knowledge such as written documents and blueprints and tacit knowledge such as routines. Also, it has been revealed that firms that do a better job of protecting their unique knowledge from being misused, undergo any theft, observed, and imitated, will always have an advantage over those that don’t have these characteristics. It can be argued that it is essential to pay more attention to strategic resources of firms and the way they are managed, more important being the ones having capability of providing economic value and competitiveness to the companies in the era of globalization and global outsourcing. Keeping in view this fact, it can be very well concluded that human resources and knowledge resources are the most valuable assets for organizations and an effective management of those assets boosts companies for an effective management of these assets to leverage knowledge and skill sets of employees. Conglomerates are building appropriate skill sets through various dynamic corporate strategies like acquisitions, partnerships, and tie-up, and cooperation in research and development. For example, Microsoft, Cisco, and Nokia have diversified their landscape through acquiring start-ups with Internet, software, human resource, knowledge management, outsourcing, technical and content capabilities.

Over the decades, the technological knowledge–based changes have led to changes in communication systems, transformation in knowledge management, digitalization of content, and social networking of knowledge, and now the world is moving toward global knowledge management system platforms like symbiotic web where humans and machines can interact in symbiosis.

2 Knowledge view of the firms

Knowledge is viewed as the most special strategic source that does not decline the way economic and other factors of production can; however, it increases exponentially through strategic planning and results in ultimately increased turnover of the firms. Most of the knowledge-based resources framework is intangible, dynamic, and lead to unique development through path dependency that forms the basis for economic rent creation and knowledge-based view of firms. The essential elements of knowledge-based view can be summarized as follows:

3 Knowledge management and web

In the era of globalization, the ability to manage knowledge is becoming increasingly more crucial. The creation and diffusion of knowledge are considered as important factors in competitiveness. Hence knowledge is being regarded as a valuable commodity that is embedded in products and in the tacit knowledge of highly mobile employees. Although knowledge is a valuable commodity or an intellectual asset, it possesses some paradoxical characteristics that are unique from the other valuable commodities. Knowledge cannot be treated as one thing, but rather as a diverse range of subjects which can be useful to someone in some situations. Companies are making their choices regarding the scope of programs and problematic within knowledge management. A three-part framework for categorizing the different types of KM initiatives cab be summarized as:

  1. External structure initiatives (e.g., gain knowledge from customers, offer customers additional knowledge).

  2. Internal structure initiatives (e.g., build a knowledge-sharing culture, create new revenues from existing knowledge, capture the individual’s tacit knowledge, store it, spread it, and reuse it, and measure knowledge-creating processes and intangible assets produced).

  3. Competence initiatives (e.g., create careers based on KM, create microenvironments for knowledge transfer).

The philosophy of KM 3.0 is focused not only on the individual; rather, it focuses on the use of knowledge. The advent of the Internet and the World Wide Web has made unlimited sources of knowledge available to us all. The growth of information on web has mounted with wider range of technologies, the information is sometimes not even recognized, considered to be complete and many times found inaccurate as well, making its access, acceptability, and usage not worth.

Therefore, to fit into this framework of knowledge management, the direction of which interlinks the identification, regeneration, processing, dissemination and applicability of knowledge to increase efficiency, growth and profitability in organizations seems to make advent of timeline of Web, Web 3.0. The major application areas of knowledge management are given in Table 1.

Table 1:Application areas of KM.

1 Globalization of business
2 Leaner organizations
3 Corporate amnesia
4 Technological advances

4 Landscape of knowledge management

As there are different schools of thoughts for empiricism and rationalism pertaining to knowledge based on prior knowledge, experience, innate thoughts, perception, induction, the concept is more rational and useful in application for human behavior, desire, attitude, trust and interpretations. The thought process from various schools of thoughts has been used in knowledge framework, for analyzing it in a logically rigorous manner.

When we talk of constructing knowledge creation for an organization, a school, or a classroom, any platform of teaching or learning science needs to consider the nature of knowledge to be taught. Recent studies emphasize that scientific knowledge practices may not be differentiated in simplistic unitary way; that is, there is no single aspect of science, and that there are some hardcore commitment practices associated with scientific practices and knowledge without any implication for science. Epistemologists by and large least focus on procedural or acquaintance knowledge, however instead preferring to focus on propositional knowledge.

The school of thoughts may differ in the types of knowledge, so mutually propositional knowledge may claim to have different things with different variations.

Epistemologists relate themselves with a number of tasks that may be sorted into two categories.

  1. First, we must determine the nature of knowledge that someone must be able to judge the knowledge by a person, he succeeds in understanding or fails.

  2. Secondly, we must be able to determine the extent of knowledge that how much, how can, how far we can know. The concept also emphasizes our testimony of others, our senses, our reasons, causes and resources to acquire knowledge.

Even if we restrict ourselves to active usages, there exist multiple senses of Knowledge and it may be imperative to differentiate between them. The difference in individual knowledge and collective knowledge can also be made. Social epistemology is the area of epistemology that addresses the way that groups, institutions, or other collective bodies might come to acquire knowledge.

Knowledge and innovation: Innovation begins with construction of new kind of knowledge within the firm; this means that business re-organization/re-orientation is an essential precursor to innovation. Knowledge management has a significant role in identification and front-end market management. Organizations appoint chief knowledge officer to manage company’s knowledge in the similar fashion in which CFO manages the capital. Without a formal system and a well-designed hierarchy, it’s not possible to manage this process. Innovation and knowledge are nowadays two key activities for organizations. Innovation is one of most vital components in a knowledge-based economy. Both innovation and knowledge go simultaneously to create customer demand and new market areas.

5 Knowledge by acquittance vs description

While clearly distinguishing between knowledge by acquittance and description [20] mentions former having cognitive relation, being aware of the object ourselves while latter being constituting relation of judgement. The distinction between knowledge by acquaintance and knowledge by description is arguably a critical component of classical or traditional version of foundationalism in real senses. This is important as this may lead to illumination of the role that is played by acquaintance in securing knowledge, which in any case requires a justified belief and this may be the most supporting role when we fall short of knowledge [10].

Table 2:Acquaintance vs description.

Knowledge by acquaintance Knowledge by description
Relation of direct awareness Ambiguous, indefinite
Without any process of inference Involves process of inference, description
Non-judgmental, non-conceptual, Descriptive only
Representational in Nature Application of concepts
Creator of real genuine relation Creator of judgmental descriptions

Many experts have discussed that experience is the real provider of immediate “knowledge of acquaintance,” “knowledge about” is the result of the systematic process that removes the independent and circumstantial contingencies of experience and extracts the principles that lie behind the “knowledge of acquaintance.”

5.1 Explicit and tacit knowledge management

Explicit knowledge consists of facts, relationships, rules, and policies that can be easily codifiable in electronic form and shared without need of for discussion, while tacit knowledge, however, defies recording. This encompasses personal skills and its transfer requires face-to-face contact or even apprenticeship. The features are as displayed in Table 3.

Table 3:Comparison explicit vs tacit knowledge management.

Features Explicit Tacit
Organizational results High-quality, uniform solution to most of the problems Appropriate, creative solutions, future opportunities exploitation
Risks and costs Current risks and costs Future costs and risks
Knowledge management Codification, classification of explicit knowledge reuse Identification of relevant and helpful staff
Targeted professionals Teamwork, innovators group formation High paid innovators, creative solution provider, one-to-one training for mentoring
Tools and techniques Data analysis, policy documents, guidelines, risk assessment tools Emails, meetings, workshops, video conferencing, road shows, coffee areas, case meetings
Source of knowledge Professional analysts Creative workers
Level of IT knowledge Multiple knowledge investment reuse Medium-to-low-level investment
Staff incentives Staff adherence to polices recognitions, rewards Emphasis on experts and original solutions
Organizations Mainly service industry, customized good provider Consultancies, product teams

The link within the explicit and the tacit is evolutionary in that the selections made by individuals are settled in or out according to their utility in a particular historical and economic reality, and eventually embedded in organizational routines which then shape and constrain further individual choices.

5.2 Attitude toward inclusion

Knowledge vs Experience: The most successful inclusion programs are the ones where people are involved and influenced by the giver. Memory research of the past has tended to concentrate on memory performance, and to neglect memory as conscious experience. This neglect may reflect the acceptance of a tacit aspect that behavior, knowledge, and experience are closely correlated. The construction of scientific knowledge is a slow, continuous process of transformation taking place over a long period of time, involving sequential, uninterrupted estimations of becoming everyday knowledge. Leaning through knowledge takes mainly three scales or parameters for changes:

  • In most of the common cases, the gainers, knowledge learners, add on their already existing theories and practices.

  • In other cases, any cognitive shock, experience, happening all put together lead the learner to deviate knowledge bank, attitude, and thought process.

  • In the third case, although rare, assumptions are transformed by way of seeing, conceiving, and talking about from everyday knowledge.

Based on above, there are three implications for interactive designer.

The same can be summarized as,

It can be interpreted from above that only a part of knowledge that is specialized can exist explicitly as information; the rest is drawn from the community as a practice of disclosure. The importance of time, social parameter, and values change when it is being considered that the different methods experimented by scientists to gain knowledge. Properties of scientific knowledge arise from the social practices enacted by specific scientific communities. Discourse processes transform prior knowledge into refined concepts that can be applied consistently by members of the scientific community. Scientific knowledge is something like a polished commodity, for which prior knowledge supplies the raw materials and social interaction supplies the tools. Prior knowledge may be a multidimensional construct that when addressed independently does influence individuals’ search for vacation information; and source credibility is the strongest predictor of type of information sources used [6]. The prior knowledge that an entrepreneur possesses plays a critical role in identification of entrepreneurial opportunities. Opportunity recognition is achieved through prior knowledge and prior experience.

Technology must understand relevant prior knowledge in a target user population to ease out its adoption. Prior knowledge is the most common factor for success and the most effective usage and technology has recently begun to explore the way challenges have to cope up aging population [13]. The boundary between the explicit and tacit types of knowledge is both porous and flexible, so there is traffic between the domains. The firm provides a special background wherein both explicit and implicit bodies of knowledge are both selected by mutual interaction to the external economic reality and then stored in the routines available to future generations of employees. Over time the quality of the interaction involved in explicit and evolving implicit types of knowledge may lead to further improvements and, thence, to superior firm performance.

5.3 Constructing scientific knowledge

There is another view that knowledge cannot be transmitted but must be constructed by the mental activities of learners on perspective of mental health. Not only personal but social perspective on learning as well as perspective on nature of scientific knowledge to be learnt is essential for presenting scientific learning in a formal setup. The organizational psychology is one of the biggest contributors of international cooperation and development of projects in multinational organizations and among professional community. Highly cited scientific production occurs within the communities only and the cohesive collaborations of similar knowledge domain and across communities. Organizational psychology may contribute to the implementation of in preview of social identity, manager adhere to professional identity, knowledge creation and accommodate professional norms and values which shapes up a knowledge led society. Ultimately management also efforts toward developing a knowledge-based identity where creativity and expertise hold important position.

6 Review of literature

Knowledge management diversified as a visible management topic by the mid-1990s as the topic gained momentum in the IT industry, application in computer services, and IT systems. During the last decade, although it has gained high popularity and visibility, with the advent of new technologies and new programs the area has come in for wide scrutiny as there are questions on the success of many of its applications, attempts, and especially those from the pure IT fields [8]. However in argument to this thought, many management experts have portrayed a new version of knowledge management as a field that evolves with the upcoming generation that both resolves the limitations of the last generation and brings more changes with new features that are beneficial to all.

There has been an exponential growth of online platforms, virtual areas of IT applications worldwide that are making it a tougher game for IT organizations to encourage a collaborative approach in sharing and leveraging their intellectual assets [1]. New-age technologies such as artificial management, IoT, and cloud computing have developed an interest area among organizations to develops such technologies of knowledge management alignment with the next generation with focus on age technologies. Knowledge management is still a young discipline and can be viewed in four emerging and complementary ways: ontological, epistemological, commodity, and community aspects [7] As knowledge management is an emerging, diversifying, and evolving stream that changes its applications, platforms, and features with emergence of every new generation, therefore, it’s very natural to have even the contradictions in different aspects. Further adding to this are opinions that the challenges to knowledge management can be put in framework engaging, exploring, experiencing, emerging, enabling and evolving aspects of knowledge [12].

Knowledge management can be treated as a subject with more interlinks with literature, discussion, planning, and a part of action, and it is mandatory that for effectively implementing a strong and knowledge-based strategy, the organization must have strong knowledge pool; this is the primary success condition of knowledge implementation in an organization [2]. Despite such criticality, knowledge management remains an ill-defined term, as many other theories, technologies of management re-orient its application and part from this some technical theories here no categorization is being found are forcibly put under this stream. It is empirical to mention that the wider range of knowledge management has assisted organizations to have better insights into knowledge options, like applications and technologies in the related field. Various knowledge theories can be viewed for the totality and complexity, as it offers a wider range of tools and techniques. Knowledge theories can provide a framework within which an organization can balance its knowledge focus and establish its strategic knowledge direction with effective communication.

While relating leadership with knowledge,it is becoming mandatory and need of the hour for leaders of successful organizations to constantly search for better ways to improve performance and results which are knowledge-based [21]. In addition, past disappointments due to various management-driven initiatives have motivated managers to gain understanding into complex issues such as knowledge, which is a key governing area of any organizational effectiveness. Knowledge management is such a new aspect that it covers most of the cross-functional areas and has become a fashion in modern enterprises. The enterprises must create and sustain a balanced intellectual and human capital resource portfolio so that they remain competitive and successful on a long-term basis. The leadership in the organization must set broad and integrated goals for managing intellectual resources and it requires systematic knowledge management. With knowledge as the major leadership force behind the “generation of ideas,” one can imagine that the emphasis must be:

  • knowledge creation

  • knowledge development

  • organizational knowledge

The above trio will continue to be the prime focus for improving society.

There have been books, journals, and articles on knowledge management systems, but all the information available is in the form of surveys only [7]. Nevertheless, knowledge management systems (KMS) are the tools and techniques that support knowledge management practices in organizations, but only surveys and data interpretation cannot present this wide area as a cumulative knowledge bank. There must be some technologies, current landscape, content, tools that provide a framework for research into the development and use of these systems in organizations and the technologies only support the knowledge transformation hugely and future trends are also based on them only [5]. The explicit knowledge contributes strongly to technology solutions along with search and classification. The formation and communication of tacit knowledge, along with making it explicit, are some of the major some encouraging developments in knowledge transformation process. The attention toward the new term technology-enhanced learning (TEL) and related it to knowledge management (KM) emphasizing it to be an important means to increase individual and organizational performance and further highlighting the traditional KM and TEL models have failed to cope with the fast-paced change and critical challenges of the new knowledge era [14]. Over the past few years, the learning management system (LMS) has become an essential element of TEL. The emergence over the past couple of years of Web 2.0 technologies has provided new opportunities for alternative TEL solutions, often referred to as TEL 2.0 or E-Learning 2.0. Referring to Web 2.0 on social networking and collaboration between the synthesizer and the user while Web 3.0 as an intelligent web or semantic web with technologies like big data, linked data, cloud computing, 3D visualization, to make passive learner into active learner in the learning process [15], describes, Web 2.0 was transformation from web sites to social networking. In addition, it comes with features such as, computing platform, desktop applications replacement with web applications, attributes like network as platform, continuous updated software, and consuming and remixing data from various sources. It can be concluded that Web 2.0 is entirely a learner-centric activity; therefore, teaching and learning process got amalgamation. The predictions of Web 3.0 vary due to the variations in the technologies which will make up Web 3.0 in its formative years. On the contrary [18] has challenged Web 3.0, e-learning 3.0: the first major challenge is due to the nature of Web 3.0, its vastness, interoperability, lack of server-side checks, less privileges control, and increased privacy and security risk; the second major challenge is the wider application of Web 3.0, if current industry, education scenario will be able to utilize the benefits of Web 3.0 when the struggle with the previous generations is still going on.

To stay competitive, dynamic, and strategic, it is critical to stay updated with the technology trends as the time progression happens, the maturity of web happens as well in its own unique way [19]. Web 3.0, which is a unique web experience where machine will be able to understand and interpret data in a similar fashion as human being emerged from Web 2.0 which was interactive Web and which in turn emerged from Web 1.0 that has only static informative characteristics [19]. Experts have further revealed that Web 3.0 is a worldwide data warehouse where any format of data warehouse can be shared and interpreted by any device over any network, but the evolution of Web 3.0 will bring forth new opportunities and challenges. Autonomous integration of data and services which increase the preexisting capabilities of Web services, apart from creation of new functionalities, is the major opportunity, while unauthorized access and manipulation of data, autonomous initiation of action [18, 19], and the development of harmful scripts and languages would continue to be the major challenge in this technology. However, as a precaution, for anyone in the data information industry, data management expertise needs to be ready and acquire knowledge arising from Web 3.0 technologies. Further, as Web has been running into the era of Web 3.0 equipped with the development of semantic technology, therefore, the management of enterprise that suits Web 3.0 becomes a key addressal area for modern organizations. In this dynamic business environment, an emerging generation of users based on social networking experiences and empowered to create, publish, and redistribute content may find the structures of the Web 2.0 services a user-centered approach [4]. Innovative solutions would be required in this shift of web environment, innovative approaches to training, new emphasis on the role of resources as learners in changing business environment and rethinking the underlying architecture Web based technologies [4]. The continuous evolution of the Internet has thrown opportunities and challenges in web-based education and learning [17] and the traditional version of web, that is, Web 1.0, started as a read-only medium; the next version Web 2.0 established itself as read/write medium. Now the currently evolving version of web, namely, Web 3.0, is said to be a technologically advanced medium which allows the users to read/write/execute and also allows the machines to carry out some of the thinking so far expected only from the human beings. In a short time, Web 2.0 and now Web 3.0 have created new tools and technologies for facilitating web-based education and ramp; learning [3, 17].

As Web is the fast publication research medium all time, organizations consider Web-based technology as the most asset for income generation and cost control in the organizations [3].Web 3.0 creates the potential for implementation of new strategies and new forms of professional approaches and today, it is possible to assume the trends of Web 3.0 development [3]. New organizational strategies require both awareness of new opportunities in networking and acceptance and understanding of new organizational strategies of managers. The evolution of Web 1.0 to Web 3.0 has divided the education world into [9]:

  • digital natives

  • digital immigrants

Digital immigrants utilize traditional classroom pedagogy and communication methods in the classroom They went to school and graduated without Google or Wikis and have closely witnessed this epoch of technology revolution in education. In this sense, digital immigrants primarily include educators, educational administrators, parents, and lifelong learners who have been forced to adopt technology for education and learning.

It can be concluded from the above views of experts that the evolution of the Web 1.0 to Web 3.0 in the last three decades has had a tremendous impact on the growth and the future of education and e-Learning. Web 3.0 or the “Semantic Web” builds on Web 2.0 or the “Social Web” which is characterized by learner interaction and participation on the web. As the third generation of the web, Web 3.0 uses data from learner interaction on the web to generate and deliver customized content to the learners. Computer algorithms form the basis of Web 3.0 which makes it possible for machines, tools, and application to exhibit artificial intelligence (AI).

7 Web transformation

The Internet was invented by Tim Berners-Lee in 1989, with the vision of communicating or sharing information in a common information space, called web. By the exponential growth, it became a medium to store and access various versions of learning contents such as text, pictures, audio, and video. The main challenge of Web 1.0 is the creation of the content and sharing with others. The users are not able to curate knowledge. As a result, the Web 1.0 is widely recognized as read-only web with its general attributes like static information and minimal interaction between web sites. As per sources, Web 2.0 is a transition from web sites. Added to that, it is a full computing platform, replacing desktop applications with web applications, with features like network as platform, continuous upgraded software, and consuming and remixing data from various sources. Digital Natives were able to curate content and it demands the learner to participate actively in the learning process. Web 3.0 predicts that online content is organized in a semantic way, much more personalized for each user, intelligent websites and applications, and search and behavioral advertising. Integration of Web 3.0 technology into KM 3.0 system to collect, share, and use valuable knowledge to help people get their job done. Therefore, KM 3.0 is about having knowledge we can use rather than collecting and sharing as much as possible [11].

The characteristics of all forms depicted in Table 4, as

Web 1.0 Web 2.0 Web 3.0
Read Read and write Read, write, and
execute

HTML portals XML, RSS RDF

8 Knowledge transformation

Many organizations accumulate and created large knowledge systems, expert systems, knowledge repositories, and intranets to ensure the availability of their knowledge assets. Without having a clear strategy for using this knowledge the companies at least made sure that they collected it and kept it in a database or file somewhere. KM 2.0 focused on sharing knowledge using web-enabled and social media tools: With the origin of Web 2.0, focus shifted toward sharing, communication, and collaboration of knowledge. The popularity of social media and web tools has started to reach the enterprise and the use of these tools in an enterprise setting is often named “Enterprise 2.0” [11]. The focus of KM 3.0 lies on using existing knowledge to help people gets their job done: this simply means having access to useful knowledge whenever required. In the research by [11] knowledge worker has been equated to an island – where there is less common knowledge and more specialized knowledge. It shows that the worker must collect more for themselves, and less for others. Each knowledge worker is also supposed to be responsible for reducing the noise and the information overload by focusing more on sharing quality than quantity [11].

Table 5 discusses the differences between KM 1.0, KM 2.0, and KM 3.0 [11, 16]

Table 5:The difference between KM 1.0, KM 2.0, and KM 3.0.

KM 1.0 KM 2.0 KM 3.0
Techno-centric People-centric Productivity-centric
Command and control Social Practical
Centralized systems Decentralized ecosystems Personal and decentralized systems
Email, newsletters, databases Social tools (blogs, wikis, IMs) Practical, personal, social,
KM is extra work KM is part of my work KM is helping me do my work

Source: http://www.tlainc.com/articl256.htm [11, 16]

9 Discussions

We live in an era of information society which is based on information opportunities, where the object is not simply knowledge but the possibility of accessing it, producing, and diffusing new knowledge through global information media which a user can share, read, and writes through computers connected with internet web pages. Partial or exclusive focus on people, technologies, techniques, and systems may not enable firms a long-term sustainability but interaction between technology, techniques, and people can enable an organization to manage knowledge effectively. “Learning by doing” and “application-based management” kind of environment sustain organizational competitiveness. With these objectives, during the last few years knowledge management has extended to the adoption of technology, Web-based advancements, multiple-sensor systems, semantic web where computer is generating and thinking new information rather than humans. However, the transformation process of Web 1.0 to Web 3.0 is also accompanied with some of challenges like lack of data standard for controlling metadata and data privacy, resource description framework (RDF), web ontology language (OWL) used unified resource identifiers (URI) to represent data which can be held in database without specifying any access policy or trust boundaries. There can be chances that these challenges may make Web 3.0 vulnerable and attackers may also falsify the data internationally, and wrong coding, false services, and unwanted interpretation can happen. Data privacy is one of the most important key issues in Web 3.0. Although social and semantic Web have grown separately, both have a combined approach to leverage crisis management, formal reasoning capabilities, and decision-making in emergency situations. Nevertheless, it is unquestioned that the enormous pool of user-generated information contains pieces of highly relevant, applications, techniques, solutions, and information that are not known to the decision maker to the full extent.

Sharing of feeling using Web 3.0 technologies, creating, processing thoughts and ideas instead of linking documents only. Social web is considered as an efficient and attractive way of connecting people around the globe. The platform also allows people to find the information at a much deeper and extensive level with the meaning of the search terms and the context in which they are used. Web 3.0 enables the information in a way that machines can read it and understand it as much as humans can without ambiguousness. Today’s generation live in a virtual world, participating in group activities. Web 3.0 provides applications that are fragmented together, are in cloud, and can be run on any device very fast, very customizable, and distributed virally. It has been witnessed that manifest in Web 2.0 and Web 3.0 has been a great extension of Knowledge Management, a true communal medium the distinction between professional, semi-professional and consumers has got aligned creating a network effect of business and applications.

The future generation of web is gearing for the next great phase of Web, where not only people (Web 1.0 and Web 2.0) or machines (Web 3.0) are connected but objects like objects toaster, to a car, our keys, our phone, books, geysers, and lights fans, the list is endless, the term coined as Web 4.0. It offers a new model of user interaction with the most comprehensive and personalized, not limited simply to display information, but proposes to behave like an intelligent mirror that concrete solutions to what the user needs.

The Web is continuously evolving. The access to the Internet is not made exclusively by humans, but also from other objects, devices, and vehicles, originating the concept of Internet of Things. These new concepts and demands have led to the birth of a new Web model, entitled Web 4.0.

The concept of Web 4.0 is quite complex and changeable. Till now, there is not a mutual consensus regarding a concrete definition of Web 4.0, contrary to previous wide-accepted definitions of Web 1.0, Web 2.0, and Web 3.0. However it can be said that Web 4.0 will now craft the landscape for future generations with its unique applications such as social networks and technologies such as Internet of Things, Big Data, AI, and M2M that will play a key role in the adoption and implementation of Web 4.0, the new Web that fosters experts for further research in the area.

10 Study implications

It is seen that to gain competitiveness and to improve business processes, number of organizations have included KM as a business strategy. It is also noticed that reports of successful KM implementations have resulted in financial savings, increased revenues, and increased level of user acceptance. The researchers found that sharing tacit knowledge is key for mentoring success, and express concern that remote learning, which is becoming more common during the COVID-19 pandemic, may not be an effective means of transferring knowledge such as tacit knowledge. Also, models help us to put the disparate pieces of a puzzle together in a way that leads to a deeper understanding of both the pieces and the ensemble they make up. KM models are still new to the practice or business of knowledge management, and yet they represent the way ahead. It requires a solid foundation of understanding of what KM is and what the key KM cycle processes are, and how these fit into a model that enables us to interpret, to establish cause and effect, and to successfully implement knowledge management solutions. The Web begins to bring more knowledge and action capacity for its users, resulting in considerable changes in several aspects of daily life. Web 3.0 is moving fast toward a more dynamic and faster-changing environment, where the democratization of the capacity of action and knowledge can speed up business in almost all areas. So, there should be some solution to transfer tacit knowledge using Web 3.0 or Web 4.0. Now Web 4.0 will be the Internet of things or we may name it as a web of intelligence connections digitally.
