DNA methylation profiling reliably distinguishes pulmonary enteric adenocarcinoma from metastatic colorectal cancer. Modern Pathology, 32, 855–865. →

Knowledge Management: A Driver for Boosting Intellectual Capital

Purvi Pujari
Priyeta Priyadarshini

Abstract

The future belongs to the knowledge-based economies. A country’s growth potential will be increasingly dependent on the knowledge potential and management of the knowledge processes by the same. As the globe gears toward a circular economy, the aspects of sustainability and innovation become very crucial. The need of the hour is to focus on intellectual capital (IC) as a key driver of the knowledge-based economy. In an economy, the focus should be on creating and developing knowledge intensive industries. The method is to prioritize the boosting of IC and enhance value creation in the economy. The correct emphasis on knowledge management (KM) in an economy can be leveraged through boosting IC. This will lead to a more sustainable and innovative nation moving toward a circular economy. For any organization to ensure sustainable competitive advantage it is imperative to weave KM in its human resource initiatives. Investing in KM is a long-term strategy. Technology plays a crucial role in this whole process. KM technologies are information technologies that can be used to facilitate KM. The aim of this chapter is to identify the value creation process while providing suggestions for strategic interventions for a fruitful relation between an organization’s employees, suppliers, customers, and other stakeholders. This process needs interlinking of technology with the human resource processes. This chapter is divided into three main parts. The first part focuses on defining and explaining types of KM wherein the importance of KM systems is detailed. The second part deals with describing IC, its significance and basic components. The third and most important part deals with the relationship between KM and IC and suggests strategies for boosting IC. As the tertiary sector becomes more and more important and Technology 4.0 becomes the key driver of every growth activity, knowledge acquisition and KM will be the game changers.

Keywords: Knowledge Management, Intellectual Capital, Technology 4.0, Strategy,

1 Introduction

The move from a traditional economy to an information-based economy requires a consistent focus on knowledge management (KM) in society. The organizations in any society have to emphasize value creation through an impetus to intellectual capital (IC). KM is a deliberate process of identifying, structuring, maintaining, and exchanging the expertise and experience of employees within an organization. It is an intricate process of value creation out of the knowledge potential of an organization. It involves several elements like human resources practices, technology, culture, and organizational structures. There have been numerous attempts to define KM. KM is defined as “the process of creating, sharing, using and managing the knowledge and information of an organization [5]. It refers to a multidisciplinary approach to achieve organizational objectives by making the best use of knowledge.” KM is the name of a concept in which an enterprise consciously and comprehensively gathers, organizes, shares, and analyzes its knowledge in terms of resources, documents, and people skills. KM draws from existing resources that your organization may already have in place-good information systems management, organizational change management, and human resources management practices.

IC has been defined as “Intellectual capital is the term given to the combined intangible assets which enable the company to function.” IC is the intangible value of a business, covering the competencies of its people (human capital), the value relating to its relationships (relational capital), and everything that is left when the employees go home [2]. The focus of this chapter is to explore the role of KM in boosting IC.

KM is becoming the key function to enhance the sustainable competitive value of an organization. It refers to the learning and development of a company or its clients. It consists of a cycle of information formation, sharing, structuring, and auditing to optimize the efficacy of an organization.

Knowledge management has the following knowledge-focused activities:

  1. Generation of new knowledge or information

  2. Acquiring important information from external sources

  3. Utilizing available knowledge and information in intelligent decision-making

  4. Implanting knowledge in product and services

  5. Constituting knowledge and information in databases and respective software

  6. Assisting in the growth of knowledge through culture and motives

  7. Conveying information of the current organization into other parts of the organization

  8. Impact of KM

The importance of KM is that it contributes maximum to the development of any organization and nation as a whole. Currently, the world is observing an information explosion. Knowledge allows the creation of abilities that determines the capacity to do things in an effective manner. Knowledge allows making decisions, predictions, and associations.

Knowledge is the main source of decision-making, planning, design, and evaluation. Knowledge is also important expertise which creates a difference to individuals, groups of employees, and organizations. Generating, imparting, and disseminating knowledge or information is critical and it should be handled with care.

There are seven knowledge levels in knowledge management:

  1. Knowledge of customers

  2. Knowledge of people/employee

  3. Knowledge of products and services

  4. Knowledge of processes

  5. Organizational database

  6. Knowledge of relationships to deal with customers, suppliers, and stakeholders

  7. Knowledge about IC of the workforce

Steps in KM process:

The KM process comprises following three important areas:

  1. Accumulation of knowledge – There are various models which have been created for accumulation of knowledge. Every organization must ensure the cooperation of employees in mind while selecting the models. Many a times, employees are resistant to change and are not willing to cooperate. Knowledge accumulation is a gradual process and has to be done in stages. All resources within the organization must be thoroughly studied to ensure a comprehensive knowledge acquisition. The more important aspect for any organization is to prioritize its requirements and select the suitable technique.

  2. Storage of information – Any KM system must have the proper selection of its devices and other technology parameters. The capturing of knowledge becomes a significant step where the pathways and systems in the organizational memories are defined. This is also known as institutionalization of the knowledge.

  3. Sharing of knowledge – This is an area which specifically deals with distribution of knowledge. In this process, the interaction of employees among themselves and other stakeholders plays a very important part. The inherent culture of the organization is also very significant in ensuring the successful encompassing of the knowledge.

The main goal of KM is to initiate a learning culture and empower organizational learning. Sharing of knowledge is also encouraged among the employees. There are mainly three types of knowledge that need to be considered while we discuss KM:

Types of knowledge:

  1. Tacit knowledge – This knowledge is inborn and natural. It is not easily codified and sharing is difficult. Examples of such knowledge are creative thinking, analytical thinking. This knowledge is gained usually from personal experience and it is considered as difficult when it comes to sharing knowledge.

  2. Explicit knowledge – It is easy to share, understand, teach, and articulate. Examples include solving a mathematical equation, how to download an application in android.

  3. Implicit knowledge – It is nothing but the application of explicit knowledge. The skills which can be transferable from one work to another are an example of implicit knowledge.

2 Types of knowledge management

There are basically four types of knowledge management, which are as follows:

  1. Internal KM – It is a method of giving the staff admittance to information in order to help them to manage their responsibilities better or to improve overall information. This can incorporate different kinds of instruments and approaches – intranets, tool boxes, information bases of exploration or exercises learned, networks of training, information sharing occasions.

  2. Information dispersal – It is the process of creating information and making the hierarchical information as generally available, or known to the outside world particularly to the stakeholders. For associations with a solid base either in research, or in practical experience, needs to disseminate the information which is broadly accessible, open, and utilized as could be expected under the circumstances.

  3. Information facilitation – It is the process of associating the stakeholders with significant information and expertise. Here the job is to help interface advancement accomplices and partners with the information they need, regardless of whether it comes from internal or external sources.

  4. Building information limit – It is the process of building the limit of organizational clients to create, get, offer, and use information viably. This may be the most testing yet additionally most principal approach to put information at the administration of improvement.

3 Components of intellectual capital

  1. Human capital – The creative capacity, awareness, ability, and knowledge of employees is the human capital of any organization. Although people do not like to be denoted themselves as capital, so terms like talent and human resources are used alternatively.

  2. Relational capital – It explains the relationship of an organization with investors, customers, employees, stakeholders, and communities. This includes both formal and informal contracts.

  3. Structural capital – Company’s capacity to invent and innovate, business processes and culture contribute to structural capital. These are intangible assets of any organization. Examples include data, media, intellectual property, systems and processes.

The achievement of each organization depends increasingly more on human resources than just physical capital. At the point when we think about human resources as a mix of capability, aptitudes, capacities and insight of individuals, that implies as a factor, that gives exceptional, unique, and uncommon character to each business, at that point, we realize that specifically, representatives are the source that can learn, ready to make changes, development, and willingness to have innovative exertion, which directly can lead to long-term flourishing organization.

IC is a term that refers to the intangible assets of an organization, its employees, and their cumulative knowledge and awareness about a function/job. It includes its relationships with its investors, customers, which is termed as relationship capital, its employees, employee training and development, performance appraisal which is termed as human capital, and what is left when employees go home, that is, database, policies, culture, and mission is termed as structural capital. It is a conceptual term used to try to measure assets a company may have that are not visible on a balance sheet, such as the expertise and experience of its employees.

Although this is an important component of any organization, it is subjective and very hard to quantify accurately. However, successful organizations usually focus on uplift and enhance their IC by training their employees to a certain level and making sure they are motivated and encouraged to give their best in a congenial and conducive environment. Companies that fail to take these thoughts into account may find employees who are less motivated, poorly trained, thereby affecting profits and productivity of the organization.

Hence, the focus on IC is very crucial for the process of value creation of any organization [1]. This in turn impacts the overall productivity and competitive sustainability of the company in the long-term.

4 Literature review

Researchers across the world have studied the concept of KM and its significance in boosting IC. The following literature review Table 1 suggests that KM has been identified as an effective tool in enhancing the IC of any organization.

Table 1:Literature review.

S. no. Author Take on KM & IC
1. Jan et al. [7] IC statement to be used as a communication regarding knowledge-based strategy.
2. Garcia-Perez et al. [4] The focus of this research has been on the calculation and reporting of IC.
3. Marr [9] This book gives a multidimensional perspective on the concept of IC.
4. Yusliza et al. [13] This study focuses on the green IC as a model to increase clean production in the long term.
5. Richard & James [12] This is an extensive research work focusing on the literature pertaining to IC. It underlines the significance of IC for the progress of any economy.
6. Leif & Patrick [3] This work is focused on the definition and explanation of fundamental concepts related to IC and KM.
7. Nick [11] This research work explores the concept of growth in business and IC. The performance of a company and its relation with the IC has been studied in this study.
8. Mohan & Youndt [10] The capabilities embedded in any organization have been extensively studied in this research along with the association with the IC. It also examines the innovation performance of a business as a result of enhancement in IC.
9. Antonio [1] This study states that as the earlier concept of organizational capital changes and relevance of IC takes a front seat. The organization must give more focus to report components of IC and ensure its disclosure.
10. Hussinki et al. [6] This research focuses on an organization’s performance link with its management of IC and KM.

The detailed review of the literature suggests that the pre-existing field of KM has taken a center stage with technology and human capital becoming the revenue generation sources. The disclosure of IC by the organizations is becoming crucial day-by-day [1]. There have been lots of studies suggesting the close link between IC and KM and the performance of an organization [6]. The progress of an organization is closely related to the IC assets it holds and also the way it manages them with leading technologies of KM [11]. To make this process effective, there is an imperative need to redefine the concepts related to IC and KM [8]. The connect between innovation and the business performance of the organization has been studied by many researchers. Sustainability is a new field that has been increasingly been explored by researchers all over the globe. There has been an increased emphasis on upgrading technological infrastructure.

5 Knowledge management: a driver for boosting for intellectual capital

The process of improving competitive sustainability lies strongly on the boost to IC using KM as the key driver. The focus is more and more on tacit knowledge. The working together of people, processes, and technology is a must to build a sustainable strategic business position. To ensure the process of value creation is continued in an organization, the use of technology and the existence of a clear vision is a must. The top management must foster a culture of knowledge sharing and encompassing the current technology to boost IC.

6 Strategies for boosting IC

Any organization which wants to excel in this competitive world has to leverage its strength in IC. There can be multiple ways to boost IC in an organization. Following are the strategies to enhance IC:

  1. Establishing a firm culture of knowledge enhancement: For enhancing IC in any firm, there has to be a culture of giving priority to knowledge enhancement. Establishing such a culture will require the policy guidelines from the top management which underlines the value of knowledge sharing and a boost to related competencies and skills.

  2. Establish a system to integrate ethics into your organization’s culture: Social capital of any organization cannot be improved unless and until there is the due significance given to ethics in the process of accumulation of knowledge.

  3. Create the infrastructure of KM and retention: Every organization must invest resources to establish and communicate the same to its stakeholders.

  4. Holistic development of human capital: Any process of developing IC in an organization is incomplete and ineffective unless and until it focuses on the holistic development of its employees. The structure and process of IC have to encompass all aspects of the development of a human being.

  5. Connect with organizational strategic intent: The KM infrastructure has to be up-to-date and consistent with the requirements of the organization. A strong connection has to be ensured with organizational long-term strategies and vision.

7 Conclusion

In the post-COVID-19 world, the relative significance of the IC of an organization has been proven once again. As the traditional sources of competitiveness of an organization take a back seat and relevance of IC has been reestablished. The firms must rethink and re-strategize to give a boost to their IC. The knowledge domains have to be clearly identified and should be given due importance. The companies have to identify and reinforce a well-defined strategy for boosting IC. This strategy should be designed keeping in mind the vision and ethical culture of the organization. In the coming days, the organization with a sound ethical IC will be able to withstand the turbulence in the corporate world. The future belongs to an organization with an efficient KM infrastructure. This will generate a sustainable competitive edge in the global business landscape. Intellectual and social capital will be the new currency in the days to come.

Web-Based Knowledge Management Distributed Application for Medical Certificates Using Blockchain Technology

Rupa Ch
Divya Midhun Chakkarvarthy

Abstract

Globally, disruptive technologies play a vital role in ordinary people’s lives to technocrats, small-scale industry to multinational companies, and private sectors to government sectors. Providing security and privacy preservation of the sensitive data in various applications related to a bank, healthcare sectors, etc., are becoming significant issues with advanced technologies. Unauthorized persons and also attackers get benefits from the features. Permissionless and open access features will become advantages to the cyber attackers to crack the data. These issues will be resolved by adopting some knowledge management systems (KMSs) like blockchain technology, and Artificial Intelligence. Therefore, this chapter proposed a web-based knowledge management decentralized application using blockchain technology in healthcare sectors. Especially in the healthcare centers, people’s sensitive personal data such as name, address, financial details, and cause maintain as records. Cyber attackers treat the medical records as treasure. Advanced featured technology, blockchain incorporation with web-based applications can reduce the attacks like ransomware, phishing, Brute force, steals credentials, and Man in the middle attack. The technology’s main inherited features are distributed, immutable, transparency, security, and peer-to-peer network communication. This hybrid approach, a combination of Web 3.0 and blockchain technology, increases the privacy and preservation of official medical documents issued by healthcare centers. This chapter illustrated the process of a proposed decentralized application using Ethereum-based Blockchain with Metamask wallet. This work’s main strength is the proposed application deployed results over an ethereum blockchain platform on test RPC, which are laps in the maximum related literature survey works currently available. Furthermore, presented comprehensively about the deployment cost of the application operations in terms of smart contracts and execution time for uploading credential requests.

Keywords: Web 3.0, Blockchain, Knowledge Management, Ethereum, Metamask, DApp, Official Health Documents,

1 Introduction

The Internet has developed a lot over the last three decades. Web 3.0 is an advanced version of Internet services designed as a semantic web to create intelligent and open web applications connected [1]. It includes various technologies to create real-time and user-friendly applications and make the Internet an intelligent information system [2]. In Web 3.0 technology, data is integrated in a decentralized way. Also, Web 3.0 applications use blockchain technology to make a secure database and verified communication. The advanced features of Artificial Intelligence, machine learning, data analytics, and blockchain technology such as intelligence, analytics, distributed, transparency, immutable, and security are accommodating to achieve the objectives of Web 3.0 [3].

According to WHO (World Health Organization), 95 percentage healthcare applications or centers were exploited by hackers or attackers in 2017. The primary threat to healthcare data security is ransomeware attacks [4]. Hackers tend to steal health records because they treat them as most valuable assets than physical currencies or assets. These medical records consist of a patient’s sensitive information like a name, address, financial details, phone numbers, and social security members. Moreover, as per a report of Trustwave [5], medical records worth in the black market is very high. Each health data record is valued at up to $250. Key features of Web 3.0 and blockchain technology can reduce threats or attacks on medical records.

Blockchain technology and Web 3.0 are both known as distributed ledger technology (DLT). Blockchain technology-based browser enabled the features of Web 3.0, i.e., brave browser [6]. It fixed major undermining issues in earlier versions like Web 2.0 and Web 1.0 and resolved them by adopting blockchain technology features. Blockchain is peer-to-peer (P2P)-based interconnected blocks using crypt hash values. The significant concepts of blockchain technology are mining and consensus protocols.

According to National Information Center, India, Blockchain is a KMS [7]. Because it can create the transactions, store them in a block, and process the block over a blockchain. Committed transactions are immutable by connecting them with hash values of previous blocks. The processing of a blockchain depends on consensus protocols. These protocols are using to share, manage, and transfer the data among the nodes in a blockchain. Proof of Work (PoW), Proof of Stack (PoS), and Ripple, etc., are the most standard consensus protocols [8].

Different blockchains types are available to deploy the proposed applications. Those are public blockchain, private Blockchain, consortium blockchain, and hybrid blockchain. The common similarities among all the blockchains are that every blockchain has nodes operating on P2P networks [9]. Each node maintains an own ledger that is updated frequently. Furthermore, every node verifies, receives, and initiates the transactions and also creates the blocks. The comparison among different blockchain types has shown in Table 1.

Table 1:Comparison of blockchain types.

This chapter consists of information about the proposed system, that is, a blockchain-based distributed application (DAPP), to securely create healthcare certificates and store them over an ethereum-based public blockchain [10]. To implement this application uses the etherum platform with Truffle suit and metamask wallet as mentioned in Table 1.

Related work on the proposed concept has been mentioned as a literature survey in Sections 2 and 4 has proposed system architecture with the step-by-step process of the proposed system smart contracts are as shown as algorithms. Evaluation results and analysis of the proposed system are described in Section 5. And also shows comparison of proposed work with the existing works by considering some features. Section 6 has the chapter conclusion and future scope.

2 Rationale

Availability of the information to the user level of expectations is becoming very difficult. Services are like privacy, connectivity, and accessibility of the data from everywhere is a complex task. But this can be resolved by using Web 3.0 and blockchain technology features. The main basic concepts and features of these technologies are as follows.

2.1 Web 3.0: preamble [11, 12, 13, 14]

The main features of Web 3.0 are Knowledge management like Artificial Intelligence, Connectivity, Ubiquity, Semantic Web, and 3D Graphics. Web 3.0-based application content can access by the number of applications at any time from anyplace. These applications’ scope is to search, generate, share, and analyze based on the meaning of the words instead of keywords or numbers. Web 3.0-based DAPPs as shown as follows.

Applications:

  • Brave: It is a Web 3.0-based browser. Chrome, Internet Explorer, etc. are based on Web 2.0 Applications (Apps).

  • Stroj & IPFS: Designed DAPPs using Web 3.0 features to store maximum amount of data. Like a Google drive application in Web 2.0.

  • Status: This application is used to communicate with each other using messages. It was designed using Web 3.0. Like Whatsapp, Sharechat applications which are running on Web 2.0.

  • Experty: This DAPP uses to make audio, and video call skype that was designed using Web 2.0.

  • Steemit & Aksasha: These are Web 3.0-based social network applications like Facebook and Twitter those are applications of Web 2.0.

  • Ethlance: It is a Web 3.0-based Remote Job Platform. This application has built totally on blockchain and using only cryptocurrencies for transactions.

2.2 Blockchain technology: preamble [15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20]

Key Elements: A blockchain is a group of different concepts and methods. Those are Cryptography, Hashing, P2P network, distributed ledger system, Proof of work, Merkle tree, blockchain network, block, etc. Blockchain technology’s main features are transparency, distribution, immutable, security, P2P connectivity, and decentralized. It is complicated to hack where data was not maintained in a centralized server. The main concepts of blockchain technology are briefed as follows.

  • Cryptography: Asymmetric key cryptography is used in the blockchain for providing security, authenticity, and integrity to the data and users.

  • Hashing: It is a nonreversible process that is used for encrypting the data of a block.

  • Peer-to-peer network (P2P): The nodes here are not connected to any central system. It is a secure decentralized and distributed network.

  • A distributed ledger system: Each record information is available at each node of the P2P network rather than at a central authority node.

  • Proof of Work: It is a process performed by the nodes in blockchain technology. Generally, nodes add a block into the blockchain permanently by solving a mathematical puzzle given a system.

  • Merkle Tree: Merkle tree structure saves the block space by keeping a single hash value rather than taking all the transactions.

  • Blockchain Network: It contains several connected nodes by following a P2P connection. Every node in the blockchain can do proof-of-work (PoW), verify the transactions, and process them.

  • A Block: A block is like a ledger or collection of records.

Components of a block: A block in a blockchain has the following attributes: Block header, block size, transaction data, and transaction counter. Table 2 shows the details of the characteristics of the block [21].

Table 2:Fields of a block.

3 Literature survey

Ryahei Banno et al. [23] proposed a SimBlock, which is a simulator based on blockchain. It impersonates the P2P network over a blockchain. The blockchain network is adaptable because of its architecture that has various nodes with different configurable parameters. There are no ways to validate or trace the behaviors of a blockchain. This technology adoption rate is increasing daily due to its advanced features like immutable, transparency, and maintains the data as distributed ledger manner.

Gaohe Li et al. [24] proposed a new method for verifying electronic invoices’ authentication using blockchain technology. It solves the tax and financial departments’ problems by analyzing the risks in a digital invoice system by processing computer images. Initially, the sum of image pixels and modulus values are taken from the image to verify whether the electronic invoice has been tampered with or not. The identification and tracking of invoices are made using OCR (Optical Character Recognition) technology to check whether it is authentic or fake. This system automatically identifies the invoice to avert one invoice from utilizing once more.

According to Junfeng Shi et al. [25], the most recent research is done on blockchain technology. Here proposed a life cycle model using blockchain technology for Internet-based financial products. It anticipates the products life cycle curve based on the power grids state. Here the life cycle curve is classified into three stages. It starts with the input period, then the maturity stage, and finally, the recession period. It has a high reference value which helps in developing power finance.

Daniel Tse et al. [26] proposed storing usernames and passwords in an encrypted format using blockchain technology. The users provide their details that have usernames and passwords to validate the information authentication system. Many information systems are available for accessing the data, hence unable to remember the users’ credentials. Most people forget their usernames and passwords and also facing a struggle to retrieve them again. The proposed system is using blockchain technology for storing the user’s credentials. Blockchain technology is immutable. The identity information stored would not be affected by any cyber attacks.

M. Fakhar Manesh et al. [27], provided an attempt to conduct a comprehensive, structured, and objective analysis of academic research in the Knowledge Management literature in the Industry 4.0 era. The study uses bibliometric evaluation to uncover the field’s layout and determine the direction of future research. Later, a systematic literature review has performed about the cluster results. Literature review determines the outcome of each cluster’s unique contribution. The results of this study show that quality assurance professionals need to contemplate, realize, and incorporate all aspects of “Industry 4.0” progress in their firm to reduce future adverse effects on the firm’s business. The only drawback observed is the presence of a noticeable fragmentation of some keywords in some clusters.

Maurizio Atzori et al. [28] discussed the ways of Data Exploration provided by the emerging Web 3.0 Paradigm. The proposed work significantly focuses on how Web 3.0 is different from its predecessors by having Human-Computer Interaction, Semantics, Patterns, Data Exploration, and Recommender Systems. The proposed work promotes the dissipation of the best results in all the domains related to Data Exploration in the broadest perception.

N. Iqbal et al. [29] developed an approach RIVIMS using machine learning techniques and blockchain-based smart contracts. RIVIMS refers to a reliable, intelligent veterinary information management system. This method has two segments: a blockchain-based information management system and data analytics models. Hyperledger is used to develop the information management system in RIVIMS. Data analytics and predictive models are built on a permission blockchain framework. These models intend to research the patients’ details to find out underlying styles and construct a sturdy prediction model based on the machine learning algorithms. According to the predicted results, veterinary clinics’ management can improve its future business plans, such as future resources and staff needs. This system allows the clinic to retain its customers and gives an advantage over its competitors.

K. P. Yu et al. [30] proposed a blockchain-enhanced security access control scheme to address the semi-credibility nature of the IIoT (Industrial Internet of Things) cloud platforms, and blockchain implements authentication, public keys, features of the users, and revocation lists. Domain administrators are responsible for developing security and privacy policies for the domain and implementing encryption functionalities. Intermediate decryption parameters will be returned from servers only when the features are mapped by considering the user’s ID not available in the revocation lists. This scheme helps us store securely, control, update, and delete information for business intelligence data to track and recover malicious users.

L. Da Xu et al. [31] proposed a work that inspects state-of-the-art IoT (Internet of Things) security using blockchain.This work focuses on addressing security functions, challenges, technologies, approaches, and related IoT scenarios. The proposed work utilizes blockchain keys like decentralization, consensus mechanism, data encryption, and smart contracts to build distributed IoT systems. Smart contracts are used for device registration, data storage, and service provisioning. By utilizing these features, it prevents potential attacks, as well as reduced transaction costs.

Shuchih E. Chang et al. [32] proposed an analytical review and systematic research on blockchain-based supply chains to facilitate the technology’s research. This work presents the benefits and problems prevalent in the supply chain paradigm that uses blockchain. A total of 106 review articles were examined to conclude blockchain technology usage in supply-chains. Digitalization, transparency, collaboration, traceability, and stakeholder involvement are significant issues resolving blockchain-based platforms. The integration of blockchain technology into supply chains can provide better and efficient performance, increase security, ensure distribution of governance, and help process automation. This paper presents the summary of applications of blockchain. The social impacts of these technological applications and their technical adoption/diffusion are also highlighted in this paper.

Christos Karapapas et al. [33] presented how smart contracts and IPFS (Inter planetary file system) systems be used by criminals to launch ransomware attacks. The ransomware attacks have been a significant issue for Internet users in recent times. The proposed system consists of authors and affiliates, the authors create the ransomware and store them in the IPFS, and affiliates buy this ransomware through smart contracts based on Ethereum. Then the affiliates use this ransomware to infect victims. Solidity uses for writing smart contracts, and these contracts are composed of seven functions. The IPFS offers excellent flexibility and robustness, and with the advantage of the churn of IPFS, it was possible to offer secured services for affiliates. The proposed mechanism has enhanced security and is for fellows to register and get the ransomware. It is hard to get this mechanism offline, which is its greatest asset.

Ryuya Uda [34] proposed an efficient mechanism to prevent the falsification and fabrication of video recordings by surveillance cameras. Videos recorded are signed and chained on the blockchain in this mechanism, which provides data integrity. The author proposed creating trust chains based on some predefined conditions and distinguishing cameras. The signer of the trust chain got rewarded. This mechanism proves to be efficient and effective against the fabrication of video recordings and is also cost-efficient.

Le Jiang et al. [35] have proposed a blockchain technology-based framework using OSN (Open Science Network) and DOSN (Distributed Open Science Network). It combines both because online social networking sites are becoming more and more general in today’s life. Still, people face privacy leakage problems because of the developers’ approaches like centralized data management systems, and lack of security protocols. The proposed method resolves this issue. In this work, the authors used real-time data set to evaluate the proposed system performance. The system’s advantage is to solve the users’ privacy issue, and the drawback is that it is necessary to design complicated protocols to support routing carefully.

According to G. Zyskind et al. [36], there are raised surveillance incidents cases and exploitation of the security breaches. The attackers or unauthorized parties are misusing the collected data from the breaches. In this work, the authors described a mechanism to control user’s data on their own using a decentralized approach. Here proposed a protocol to turn a blockchain hooked on a control manager with an additional feature such as automatic accessing. Here the transactions are not operated based on crypto balance. The advantage is that there is more security regarding personal data.

The main objective of this research proposed by Shu Yang et al. [37] was to improve traditional blockchain protocol’s linear structure. The authors used DAG, that is, Directed Acyclic Graph to avoid limited throughput. Here the blocks have arranged based on the widths and levels that are generated by the CoDAG (Compact Directed Acyclic Graph) structure. CoDAG provides more security than traditional blockchain.

4 Blockchain and web-based use case: decentralized application for official medical documents

Generally, any KMSs are categorized into three types such as intelligent technique-based systems, knowledge work systems, and enterprise KMSs. A web-based KMS with the advanced technology blockchain improves the user experience to capture the details quickly. Moreover, this application or system can process, organize, and share the data with all the authorized nodes in the network efficiently by following some standard strategy.

4.1 Web-based knowledge management DAPP

Knowledge management is a process for making better data decisions, quality communications with the information, and improving data agility [38]. The main components of knowledge management are process, content, users, and strategy. Figure 1 shows knowledge management-based decentralized application architecture running using blockchain technology and Web 3.0.

In blockchain-based DAPP for official medical documents, users initially request a medical document through Web application to process the given details. Metamask, a cryptocurrency wallet, asks for confirmation to process the user’s request over a blockchain. The process starts on a blockchain by following some stages whenever confirmation is received from the admin or physicians. Minors begin to mine the data by solving a mathematical puzzle before making the data as a transaction in a block [39]. In the next stage, generates a Merkle root hash value to the data. The result stores as a transaction in a block created by a minor. Finally, the block is connected with the blockchain, as shown in Figure 1.

The step-by-step process of establishing a connection between a web-based application to a blockchain network to deploy a decentralized application is shown as Algorithm 1. As shown in Figure 1, initially, either user or admin from healthcare centers must enter their credentials for integrity checking to start the web application. Activate the Metamask cryptocurrency wallet that is a web browser extension, after completing the process of validating the operator’s credentials.

Metamask acts as an interface between web applications to the blockchain network [40]. In the proposed system Ethereum-based public blockchain is using to compile and deploy the system operations. A Metamask wallet provides an Eth balance for performing the proposed system operations like creating, issuing, and verifying the medical documents on a blockchain network.

Algorithm 1:

Web-based DAPP

Input: Credentials of the users or physicians or admins

Output: Connection establishment with a blockchain network

Begin

 Repeat

  For each user ‘u’

    If ( Validate(u_ details) = = true)

      Activates web browser Extension Cryptocurrency Wallet.

      Invite for authentic confirmation.

    If confirmed

      Connection established with Ethereum blockchain

    Else

      Not connection with Blockchain network

    End If

  Else

    Not activated

  End if

 End for

Until (activate all legitimate requests on BCT)

End

4.2 Smart contracts for official medical documents

In the blockchain environment, smart contracts are lines of code where solidity programming uses to write smart contracts for a specified system’s required operations. The proposed method, maintenance of an official medical documents system, has two primary functions: issuing a user-desired medical document by authority and verifying the document by users, administrators, and physicians. Algorithm 2 shows the smart contract pseudocode of the operation to issue a medical certificate.

Algorithm 2:

Smart Contract to set the values for Issue_doc ( )

Input: Required details i.e., cause, date, name, Health_center name, doc_name for a document

Output: issue an official document interms of an unique BCT-based ID.

pragma solidity >=0.4.22 <0.6.0; // It is compiler version

contract MedicalCert

Begin

   uint public Hospital_Reg_No;

  string public Hospital_Nmae; //bytes16

  string public Doctor_Name; //bytes16

  string public Certificate_Type;//bytes32

  string public Gender;

   string public cert_Holder_Name;

   string public date_of_issue;

   string public date_of_receipt;

  string public Receipent_Name;

   uint public UID_Reciepent;

   for all the users

    function set ( variables ) public

    Begin

      Set the values to all the variables.

      End

    Return (BCT_ID)

  End for

End Contract

As shown in a smart contract, first needs to mention the version of a compiler using the instruction “pregma solidity” where considered “≥ 0.4.22 < 0.6.0” is a version of the compiler to deploy the smart contract. Later needs to mention a name to the smart contract. Here we used “MedicalCert” as the name of the proposed smart contract. As shown in algorithm 2, the document required details collect from the user by declaring them in the solidity programming-based smart contract.

Algorithm 3:

Smart Contract to Verify_doc ( )

Input: Document ID i.e BCT_ID, cert_Holder_Name,Certifictae_Type

Output: Existed or not

pragma solidity >=0.4.22 <0.6.0; // It is compiler version

contract MedicalCert

Begin

Repeat

for all the documents

function verify ( variables )

Begin

If hash (Holder_Name (BCT_ID)) = = hash (Holder_Name)

&& hash (certificate (BCT_ID) = = hash (certificate_Type)

Integrity check passed.

Else

Integrity check failed.

End If

End

End for

Until (validate all the documents)

End

Algorithm 3 shows smart contract pseudocode for the function integrity checking of the issued certificate or a medical document. In this process, verify the hash value of the received blockchain-based ID certificate with the existing documents’ hash values. Any hash value existed with the same document ID and with the same holder name considering it as a valid certificate. Here we used the Keccak-256 approach to evaluate the hash value of the required documents. Keccak 256 operates in the ethereum-based blockchain [41]. Keccak-256 in a consensus engine called Ethash. Keccak is a family of hash functions like SHA, which is used in the Bitcoin network.

5 Experimental evaluation and analysis

The proposed Web and blockchain-based DAPP for managing the official medical documents. That is implemented using Web3.js, Nodejs, Metamask, testrpc, and Ethereum-based blockchain on localhost 8545 test network. Figure 2 shows a web page for taking the user and admin credentials to operate on blockchain-based DAPP.

Metamask Wallet asks confirmation to make integrity checking of the user’s credentials. After it is confirmed, it operates by deducting some Gas (a cryptocurrency that measures in Eth) from the wallet. Figure 3 shows the fields of an official medical document the admin or physicians enter after confirming the users’ details. This DAPP performs issuing a certificate and verifying the certificate operations on top of the credentials entered into the Web page, as shown in Figure 3.

Metamsk takes confirmation to perform any operation over a blockchain network. Figure 4 shows the process of the Metamask Wallet. Figure 4 (a) shows the initial amount in a Metamask wallet, i.e., 99.8313 ETH. Figure 4 (b) shows consumption cost, i.e., 0. 004774 ETH, and a confirmation request to perform “issue a certificate” operation for the admin or physician’s credentials according to Figure 3. Figure 4 (c) shows the resultant Metamask wallet after confirmed the wallet request on the issue of a certificate. It shows the current available balance in the wallet after consumption for issue operation, i.e., 99.8281ETH.

5.1 Cost analysis

Each operation is executed on a public blockchain that is Ethereum based. Here the process refers to a transaction that takes some Gas in terms of cost to run over an Ethereum blockchain, where gas is a unit to operate. Ethereum gas paid here as Ether. Ether is known as crypto-fuel to deploy the applications over a blockchain network. A transaction cost includes the cost of execution and other expenses related to the deployment of a contract and data transfer into a blockchain. An execution cost includes smart contract internal storage and blockchain manipulation state.

Table 4 shows the costs of various operations over a blockchain network. Here, one Ether is 109 gwei units. Hence consumption costs to perform the operations have shown using ETH and Gwei. The proposed DAPP smart contract has four functions: enroll user or admin credentials, set the document credentials, issue an official document, and verify the document by all the authorized users.

Table 4:Smart contracts deployment costs.

Operations Cost (Eth) Cost (Gwei)
User credentials 157,412 0.000157412
Set document credentials 83,230 0.000083230
Issue 157,388 0.000157388
Verify 72,140 0.000072140

Table 5 shows the execution and transaction costs of the system operations. Generally, blockchain takes little time to read the data. Furthermore, transactions are validated by the blockchain nodes and make them as records into a block. Minors validated the block and appended it to the blockchain. The average time of the request and transaction processed over TestRpc-based localhost 8545 is not fixed. It varies based on the system configuration and size of the transactions.

Table 5:Execution and transaction cost analysis.

Smart contract Execution cost ( Gas) Transaction cost (Gas)
User 682,101 863,212
Admin/authority 1,587,652 2,165,431
Request 818,553 1,154,324

Figure 5 shows cost consumption by considering different “n” value: number of certificates maintenance over a blockchain. Here various cost factors represent the cost consumption for the credentials, such as Gas limit, Gas cost, Gas price in terms of CGWEI, and total Eth.

5.2 Security analysis

Analyzed the proposed Web and blockchain-based DAPP by considering two threat scenarios. Also compared with the existed systems by considering technical features and security services.

Theorem 1: Assume that any person wants to manipulate the data in the medical document illegally.

Proof:

The proposed application uses blockchain technology to maintain and issue a unique ID for every medical document. The main feature of the blockchain is immutable. If the data entered into a blockchain, it could not trace or manipulate because of connecting the data with a nonreversible hashing method. Hence, it is very challenging for the attacker to control the medical document credentials.

Theorem 2: Assume that an adversary tried to send incorrect data into a block as a transaction for maintaining over a blockchain network.

Proof:

To enroll the medical documents data over a blockchain network, uses intelligent web system in the proposed application. For that reason, validate the information by the authority before enrolling the details into a blockchain environment. There is no option to register the details by anyone other than an authorized authority. Therefore, incorrect information or fraud details enrollment can resist through the proposed supplication. Table 6 shows the comparison between proposed and existed systems.

Table 6:Comparison the proposed system performance with others.

Features
Authors		 Availability Distributed Admin Authentication Data privacy Integrity
No No No Yes Yes Yes
No No Yes Yes Yes Yes
No Yes No Yes Yes Yes
No Yes Yes Yes Yes Yes
Yes Yes No No Yes Yes
Proposed system Yes Yes Yes Yes Yes Yes

6 Conclusion and future scope

As per the fabric ventures report, Web 3.0 benefits more than 3 billion people every day with its objectives such as open, permissionless, distributed, and trustless. Web 3.0 includes the core layers of Web 2.0, such as Social, mobile, and a cloud, making it a disruptive technology. This work proposes a decentralized application using Web and blockchain technology to generate and preserve the official medical documents with security. Many critical challenges existed in the current system to issue and maintain medical records. Every medical certificate consists of the users’ sensitive data that felt like a treasure to the attackers. This chapter mainly focused on trustworthy-based access controlling the data. Only admins and physicians can operate on the medical document rather than everyone. Here, the proposed system’s performance is evaluated by considering the factors such as cost and security. This work can extend to using private blockchain in the future. And also, it needs to integrate the proposed application on real-time data sets.
