Incorporation of Indian Companies Using Integrated Web Forms as Ease of Doing Business Initiative: An Overview

Deepa Sahai Garg

Abstract

India is a leading performer in the international business setup, and its economic growth and development has made it a destination for investment for many global investors. With simplification and rationalization of regulatory framework as well as usage of information technology, it is becoming one of the fastest-growing technologically progressive knowledge economies in the global emerging markets. The knowledge-based economy has become a vital part of production, savings, investment, and consumption function which is based upon important resources like innovative ideas and intellectual capital [15]. Knowledge and knowledge management are becoming an important economic and organizational resource. The businesses have been facilitated and have grown over the last decade using Information and Communication Technologies (ICT) and various knowledge products to enable the borderless online transactions and have better global presence [23]. Corporate, legal, business and technological reforms initiated by the government have a positive impact on easing the business regulations from registration up till winding up of companies and thereby boosting investment inflows within the country.

With the intelligent web entering its third generation and offering features of semantic web, enhanced web browsing, improved information linking, artificial intelligence, machine learning, better web technologies, and resourceful searching from worldwide databases, there is a paradigm change in the world of internet and the way businesses are run, marketed, and set up are being redefined. The evolution made from Web 1.0 (read-only websites) to Web 2.0 (read-write websites) and now Web 3.0, also called the web of everything, allows user interaction on the web pages with increased connectivity in between the data. Web 3.0 makes the process of online applications more simpler and user friendly [38]. Taking advantage of this technological revolution, several business reforms have been initiated to ease the procedures relating the starting of business.

One of the important corporate reforms which has been undertaken is web-based Simplified Proforma for Incorporating Company Electronically (SPICe) Plus form as a part of Ease of Doing Business initiatives on improving the business climate for the registration of companies. The incorporation of the company form of organization by means of the advanced features of artificial intelligence using web-based filing of the application and the electronic documents and various regulatory filings have become totally paperless and are completely online on the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) web portal. Starting a business has been made lot easier, seamless through the online incorporation procedure using web-based form, that is, SPICe+ along with electronic documents like e-memorandum of association and e-articles of association [30].

This chapter elaborates and discusses the improvements brought about by the information technology revolution and its significance for the business growth and entrepreneurial development. Various initiatives undertaken by government in implementing single e-compliance window mechanism, the greater ease of starting business, major ease of doing business factors relating to the information technology solutions, important corporate reforms, the impact of newer web technologies in terms of opportunities, and challenges that such technologies will present to the corporate world are also presented in the chapter.

Keywords: Web 3.0, Knowledge Management, Ease of Doing Business, Regulation, Technology, SPICe Plus form,

1 Introduction

Any economy which is advanced and modern necessarily needs to be knowledge-driven that ensures balanced achievement of socioeconomic goals and investment growth. Indian businesses operate under globally competitive environment toward sustaining their business growth and as an attractive investment economy. The entrepreneurs in India have always struggled to start business due to burdensome policies, procedures, and regulations for the businesses. After the 1991 reforms, various changes brought regulatory changes to reduce difficulties the businesses face and providing the conducive environment. The regulatory reforms in the form of entry liberalization and privatization have had a significant impact lowering entry barriers and spurred investment in the long run, which is an important engine of economy’s growth [7]. Governments in various economies have stringent regulations that burden entrepreneurs. Such regulations not only limit entrepreneurs’ ability to operate a private business with less barriers but the entrepreneurs also start doing informal activity to avoid regulations and manipulating the laws, taking employment in other countries. Also, the investors usually do not find such economies that use excessive regulations, a destination for the investment [16]. Establishing or incorporating a manufacturing or service enterprise in India requires numerous procedural compliances, regulations, and permissions. India has approximately 12 lakh active companies. Various entry restrictions and barriers like difficulties in the start-up procedures and higher cost of doing business affect economic growth and thereby the growth of new businesses is hindered [23]. India’s regulatory procedural mechanisms are being reformed to be given new and easy forms primarily to enable the businesses to adapt to and to integrate with the international global standards and thereby attract foreign investments and encourage entrepreneurship in nation building. Efficient administration and a simple regulatory environment are highly valued by foreign investors while investing in the economies. An enabling regulatory environment provides easy entry and exit of enterprises.

The government’s Ease of Doing Business (EODB) initiative has been supportive with a noteworthy role, and the government has given foremost importance to it. Indian state governments and Union Territories have also launched several business reforms under the Business Reform Action Plan (BRAP) to provide the businesses with enabling an enterprise-friendly regulatory environment and provide the real-time ranking depending upon the reforms undertaken [10]. Ease of doing business is crucial for the entrepreneurs while setting up new businesses, large/medium/small in terms of regulatory procedures particularly relating to purchase of land, process involved in getting the property registered, and single window mechanism for obtaining the clearances along with a single online application for obtaining clearances under different acts.

The time factor, procedural formalities, regulatory hurdles, documentation requirements, and the broader restrictions for the manufacturing and service sector enterprises in the incorporation process have been simplified and made easier. Many regulatory changes have been introduced over the past years like the Limited Liability Partnership Act 2008, the Companies Act 2013, the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code 2016, and the Goods and services Tax Act 2017, along with various procedural changes and reforms These initiatives have simplified the existing regulations along with the introduction of Information and Communication Technology (ICT) to make governance and their application more well organized and effective. At the same time improved digitization of businesses along with the integration of various approvals obtained from different agencies has further reduced the number of steps of incorporating enterprise. Various business reforms undertaken have started reflecting the results. Electronic governance and technological advancements are playing a crucial role in persuading a culture of better corporate compliances and thus achieving higher and easier levels of ease of doing business. The increased usage of information technology tools assists in making the process of approval fast, effective cost wise, and completed in a time-bound manner and hence improving and facilitating the business.

2 Methodology

This chapter is of review nature exploring the research area and is based on secondary data sources. The data about the indicators for the ease of doing business, current scenario, issues, and problems have been taken from publications from Ministry of corporate affairs, PHD Chamber of Commerce and Industry of India, Niti Aayog, DIPP (Department of Industry Policy and Promotion), Department of Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT), Doing Business Report of the World Bank, different national and international publications, newspaper articles collected from various websites, and e-resources relating to research study.

3 Literature review

The review of the recent conceptual literature suggests that there has been implementation of the latest ICT-based applications in the corporate and business world to streamline the administrative and regulatory procedures related to businesses in India showing positive results.

Technological advancements in the regulatory environment and improvements in local business conditions are happening at a fast pace in many emerging markets [5]. Singh and Jaiswal [1] in their paper suggested that implementing and employing e-governance and technology will lead to filing the procedures electronically in online mode and thereby no visits to the government offices shall be required for obtaining clearances and thus reduction in the cases of bribery and corruption. The Indian government has persistently applied latest technology and web tools to streamline processes to remove the corruption practices by the business community in doing business [36]. Advanced technology should be used for ease of doing business for the enterprises to grow with minimum human interface especially in the government services and using AI should be the focus for government interface [4].

R. K. Yadav et al. [27] in their paper studied the impact of e-governance and Internet readiness on the ease of doing business. A supportive business environment promotes competition, encourages innovation, and also promotes entrepreneurship in the economy, thereby leading to economic growth [21]. Improved digital entrepreneurship is of high relevance as technological developments create many new opportunities for the emerging entrepreneurs [33]. The government that cares about its economy’s growth, well-being, and development pays more attention to friendlier laws, administrative procedures, and regulations for local small- and medium-size enterprises. Djankov et al. [32] in their paper had differentiated countries on the basis of how the countries regulate the entry of new businesses and emphasized upon the relationship between excessive regulation and its impact as the hindrance to entrepreneurship.

The governments can only function with the support of administrative procedures for collecting information like income tax, and for market activities being regulated to implement public policies. The administrative procedures worldwide seem to be increasing as governments require more information for their activities [26].

L. Alfaro and A. Chari [20] examined the effects the license raj reforms had in India on the size of the firm and the allocation of resources. The authors noted that with the easier start up rules, the number of small firms increases with increase in their performance and productivity. A reduction in resource allocation distortions over the same period and deregulation will lead to more firms and less incumbent power [3].

Simple business regulations provide the small firms the opportunity to grow, innovate, and thereby come into formal sector from the informal sector of any economy [9]. The ease by which entrepreneurs can start a business could be a strong determinant whether investors will start a business in the first instance [35]. J. Estevao et al. [19] in their paper concluded that assessing credit alone is not the major factor for knowing the suitability of any business, as there are many difficulties which are significant while dealing with the regulatory authorities. Better and minimum regulatory measures are needed, and their efficacy keeps varying over time. A supportive business environment enables the businesses to grow more and also contribute more toward the growth of the economy. The more successful the private sector is, more businesses are created, more shall be the opportunities for employment generation [35]. T. Canare [35] in their paper suggested that overall ease of doing business has a positive impact on business creation which is most strongly driven by the starting a business. In addition, the effect of the starting a business component is driven by the financial cost rather than the time and administrative cost. Finally, results change when the analysis is applied to non-high-income countries only. As per the Ease of Doing Business, 2020 report there is a significant difference between low-income and high-income economies for the ease of starting a business [7].

Goel et al. [24] in their study concluded that the technology and regulations play an important role for the country in inviting more capital flows and thereby enable more growth and development in the financial sector. Doing Business Report, an annually published study, contains the names of 10 countries that improved the most on the ease of doing business after a detailed analysis of costs and implementation of regulatory reforms and procedures in developing economies every year from 2003 onward in 190 countries, and ranks them. All the economies which are scoring high in the Doing Business Report has shown results as better entrepreneurship giving rise to better employment opportunities, improved incomes, and thereby increased taxation revenues. Also, the incidence of corruption and bribery has been seen to be reduced with the adoption of electronic means of compliance for the regulatory approvals and requirements [9]. The EODB index measured through the Doing Business Report has been recognized as an important measure among countries and as one of the important knowledge products helping in building the structure of varied regulatory reforms in several developing countries.

4 Ease of Doing Business (EODB): an introduction

Since the year 2004, the World Bank in its Doing Business Report ranks 190 countries for the ease in doing business based on several criteria called EODB ranking. The report consists of 294 business regulatory reforms spread across the 10 areas measured by doing business [28]. These reforms addressed various segments that are: starting or incorporating business, obtaining construction, or building permits, electricity connection, registering and transferring property, access to credit, protecting the interest of minority investors, payment of taxes, international trading across the borders, enforcing contracts, and resolving insolvency proceedings. The economies are scored based on their performance in 10 EODB segments (12 areas from the year 2020) included in the ease of doing business ranking to find the relative ease in their business environment [7, 9]. The EODB index is a ranking system established by the World Bank group and the ranking is based upon the simple and streamlined procedural regulations in an economy. The lower the value of EODB index, the higher is the rank for the country, indicating simpler and conducive regulatory environment for setting up businesses in that country [8]. Doing business ranking is considered an important index which is used by global investors to evaluate the investment proposals in various countries. One of the crucial Ease of Doing Business indicators is ease of starting a business which is an important indicator while assessing the Ease of Doing Business index. This indicator ranks based on all the procedures required to start a business and operate the business and the time and cost involved to complete the compliance procedures. With respect to starting a business, Doing Business Report defines various stages for the smooth establishment and conduct of any business. These stages start with formalities related to incorporating a business and employing workers, to getting the location for the business and then obtaining electricity connection, registering property, and dealing with the construction permissions [6]. Accessing finance, easy dealing with daily operations, and conveniently operating in a secure business environment are some of the crucial factors looked at while evaluating any economy.

Table 1:Ease of Doing Business indicators.

Indicator set What the indicator measures

  1. Starting a business

 The time, cost, and procedural steps along with paid-in minimum capital requirements to start a limited liability company, simplified preregistration and registration formalities, simplified post registration procedures, improved online procedures.

  1. Dealing with construction permits

 The time, cost, and streamlined procedural steps involved to fulfil the formalities regarding warehouse, time taken for processing the applications, effective quality control systems and safety nets in the system relating to construction permitting, streamlined new building regulations, improved electronic platforms or online services.

  1. Getting electricity

 The time, cost, and procedural steps to obtain electrical connection for a new warehouse, the consistency of the electricity supply, and the transparency related to tariffs, time in obtaining a new connection, online software to process applications for a connection, streamlined process of approvals through digitization processes, cost of new connections.

  1. Registering property

 Streamlining property registration process, the time, cost, and procedural steps related to registering, transparency of information and transferring a property and the quality of the land administration system taxes or fees and administrative efficiency.

  1. Getting credit

 Strengthening the rights of borrowers and lenders through collateral
laws, various credit information systems, and their depth. Improving the sharing of credit information.

  1. Protecting minority investors

 Strengthening minority investor’s protection through ease of shareholder suits, expanded shareholders’ role in company management, minority shareholders’ rights and increased disclosure requirements for the related-party transactions, extent of director liability.

  1. Paying taxes

 Making it easier to pay taxes through enhanced online and electronic system for filing and payment of taxes, total number of taxes paid, payments, time involved, and total tax and post filing processes, improved tax audit processes.

  1. Trading across borders

 Making it simple to trade across the borders through improved electronic submission and processing of documents for import and exports, time and cost required to export the product and to import, number of documents necessary to export and import, strengthening border infrastructure for imports and exports.

  1. Enforcing contracts

 Making it simplified to enforce contracts, introducing changes to the civil rules, electronic case management systems, electronic filings, cost and time involved in resolving a dispute and enforcing a contract, specialized commercial courts, alternative dispute resolution framework introduced.

  1. Resolving insolvency

 Making it simple to resolve insolvency, cost, time, and recovery rate for insolvency proceedings, new restructuring procedures, strengthening creditor’s rights.

  1. Employing workers

 Flexibility in employment regulation.

  1. Contracting with the government

 Procedures and time to participate in and win a works contract through public procurement and the public procurement regulatory framework.

5 Ease of starting business in India after technological and corporate reforms for online incorporation of companies and limited liability partnerships (LLPs)

Various economies which score high on the EODB rankings have commonalities like the usage of electronic systems for online procedures to make online incorporation a seamless process through a single window or one agency rather than multiple agencies. The top 20 ranking economies, as per the Doing Business Report, all have the facility of online incorporation, electronic portal for tax filing platforms, and online procedures for property transfers. They also have sound and transparent business regulations. Moreover, 11 economies have online procedures for permissions related to construction [7]. India has undertaken various digital efforts toward making ease of starting business seamless and to simplify the process of starting a new business. As per Doing Business Report 2020, India is among the 10 top performers and is moving closer to the international best practices on most of the indicators and implemented a one fifth of all regulatory reforms in the year 2018–19 showing impressive progression in its ranking. Over the past many decades, various initiations which had been on back seat have taken huge leaps with the technological revolution. These advancements in technologies have more effect on the ease of doing business than the number of reforms undertaken over the years in terms of liberalizing the economy.

India’s scores have improved in seven out of ten indicators and are moving closer to the international practices. India’s ranking has gone up from the 142nd position in 2014 to the 63rd in 2019 in Ease of Doing Business rankings out of 190 countries as per the World Bank’s Doing Business Report [11]. India has shown an improvement in the indicators – starting a business, dealing with construction permits, trading across borders, with the user-friendly electronic business atmosphere and the decline in the timeline [12]. Also, the wide gap between regulations and its actual implementation has reduced. To achieve this, the Ministry of Corporate Affairs has taken great initiatives for providing “Ease of starting Business” over the last few years which include recently introduced web form SPICe+ and various efforts to reduce the number of steps required for establishing business. Out of the indicators in the EODB report, there are three indicators like starting business, protecting the interest of minority, and resolving insolvency of the companies, which come in the broader regulation of Ministry of Corporate Affairs [22]. The Ministry of Corporate Affairs has been significantly contributing toward the technological improvements especially with respect to starting a business and reducing the number of procedures thereby providing enabling business environment [18].

On an average, across the countries that are part of the ranking, it takes seven procedures with twenty-five days for starting a business. When we see in New Zealand, it takes only one procedure, half of a day and almost zero fees in New Zealand for starting a business, whereas an entrepreneur must wait for 208 days in Suriname and over 144 days in Republican Bolivariana de Venezuela. As per the Doing Business Report 2020, on an average 18 days are now needed to set up any business in India and reducing it further to five days is an important agenda of government of India [7].

India started by establishing an online Value Added Tax (VAT) registration and later replaced it with the faster GST (Goods and Services Tax) registration, e-stamping, reduction in the registration fees, elimination of the minimum capital requirement, merging the applications for the Permanent Account Number (PAN), and the Tax Account Number (TAN), and enhanced the online application system features, fully integrating multiple application forms into a single incorporation form. The various other measures introduced in this respect are the removal of the minimum paid-up capital requirements, reduction in the number of procedures/steps involved, and cost for a small- to medium-sized limited liability company to start up. Various parameters like starting a new business, obtaining construction permits, and resolution of the insolvent businesses have shown noteworthy development leading to considerable improvement in the business environment in the country leading the way for the country offering global manufacturing hub to attract domestic and foreign investments. The Ministry of Corporate Affairs has contributed significantly toward the improvement of ranking in ease of starting a business like reducing the number of procedures, days, cost, and no minimum paid-up capital requirement for a company, a single form for multiple registrations, and so on. Following are some of the steps taken by the MCA as a part of the central government’s ease of doing business initiatives:

  • The minimum paid-up capital requirement for the private companies has been removed under the Companies (Amendment) Act 2015 by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs as a step toward increasing ease of setting up companies [22].

  • Under the Government Process Re-engineering (GPR) initiatives, whereby for making the incorporation process easy, smooth, seamless, and reducing the number of procedures required for starting a business, the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) had introduced “RUN – Reserve Unique Name” web service for name reservation in the year 2018. With the launch of SPICE+ and AGILE PRO forms, the RUN web service is available only for changing the existing company’s name from February 15, 2020 onward [2]. Name Availability Rules have been simplified by MCA avoiding any ambiguity in the name reservation. The rate of name rejection has dropped, and the time taken for obtaining the approval has been reduced resulting in speed, transparency, and uniformity [22].

  • Formation of Central Registration Centre (CRC) done under section 396 of the Companies Act, 2013, as an initiative under the GPR for the prompt incorporation-related services through a single online process. The establishment of CRC has been to bring the incorporation procedures parallel with the international best practices [18]. This was achieved by the launch of the SPICe and now SPICe+ form through a single integrated web form filed on MCA21 Portal. This web form provides a single window for ten different registrations or services by three central government ministries: Ministry of Corporate Affairs, Ministry of Labour, and Ministry of Finance and the State Government of Maharashtra, thereby relieving the companies from obtaining certifications under different ministries. SPICe+ tries to achieve onscreen filing and real time data validation. SPICe+ form has introduced a new user-friendly dashboard for accessing all the applications for SPICe+ and other linked forms and accessing already saved information related to SPICe+. It has two parts: Part A and Part B. Part A consists of name reservation only for new companies [2],[22].

Table 2:Part B of SPICe+ offers nine services under a single window [29,34].

S. no. Services covered
1 Incorporation of company
2 Allotment of Director Identification Number
3 Issue of PAN
4 Issue of TAN (Tax Deduction and Collection Account Number)
5 EPFO (Employees’ Provident Fund Organization)
6 ESIC (Employees’ State Insurance Corporation) registration
7 Profession tax registration (Maharashtra) w.e.f. February 23, 2020
8 Open bank account
9 GSTIN (Goods and Services Taxpayer Identification Number)

The company incorporated through SPICe+ form has to submit an application for a bank account by using a linked form: AGILE-PRO. The link to AGILE-PRO is enabled after part A SPICe+ is filled. AGILE-PRO web form has been made mandatory in all the cases. SPICe+ application cannot be submitted without this form. Many banks like Punjab National Bank, State Bank of India, Bank of Baroda, ICICI Bank, Kotak Mahindra Bank, and HDFC Bank have integrated with SPICe+. AGILE-PRO (replacing the old AGILE) is a part of SPICe +, under which application is made for Goods and Services Tax Identification Number (GSTIN), Employees’ State Insurance Corporation (ESIC) registration, Employees’ Provident Fund (EPF) registration, Profession Tax Registration (Maharashtra) and opening account in bank [14]. With the SPICe+ incorporation form, the number of procedures has been further reduced and will surely help improve the EODB ranking of India in future.

  • The requirement of a making separate application for the allotment of Director Identification Number (DIN) for first-time directors of companies has been dispensed. The bank number is provided immediately now after incorporation, resulting in saving of days to start business. Integration of the registration for GST, EPFO, ESIC, and profession tax (for Maharashtra) along with company incorporation form is another milestone achieved. Before the introduction of SPICe+ form, application for GST registration, Employees State Insurance (ESI), and EPF registration were optional along with the company incorporation in SPICe form. But now all these registrations are mandatory for a company [22,29]. Shram Suvidha portal provides for the online single window registration and licensing for various stakeholders under the Ministry of Labour and Employment. Registration of companies under the EPF and ESIC are done on the MCA’s portal through Spice+ and AGILE-PRO e-forms only at the incorporation time and not on Shram Suvidha Portal without any physical documents and physical visit. With this development the incorporation procedure has become faster. Though the introduction of this new step would require amendments under the respective EPF Act and the ESIC Act.

  • The high regulatory, very high registration fees, and start-up costs borne by the private sector make it difficult to start business and young entrepreneurs who are bootstrapped do not venture and fear incorporating new companies. Starting a business in India has become less costly due to filing or registration fees getting eliminated owing to SPICe+ company incorporation form, electronic memorandum, and articles of association. Only stamp duty charges are to be paid for the incorporation by companies and the registration fees to be charged by MCA for all incorporations with authorized capital up to INR 15,00,000 have been eliminated. As per Doing Business Report 2020, India has made starting a business enterprise less costly by eliminating filing fees for the SPICe Plus company incorporation form and for electronic memorandum of association and articles of association [18].

  • The Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code 2016, the first corporate solvency legislation, also introduced the Corporate Insolvency Resolution Process (CIRP) to provide for a time-bound insolvency resolution enabling ease of resolving insolvency [18]. IBC or the modern insolvency regime has provided a mean to form comprehensive strategy for the companies with effective tool to be able to reestablish financial capability and the creditors having better tools to negotiate and realize their money. The overall recovery rate from the creditors has increased, making India the best performer in South Asia based upon this indicator in the EODB index.

  • Under the Companies Act 2013, making a company seal or the company’s rubber stamp is no longer a legal requirement [22].

6 Ease of online incorporation of limited liability partnerships

Limited liability partnerships (LLPs) have become an important corporate entity for starting business in India, enabling and combining professional expertise along with entrepreneurial initiative. LLP is chosen due to the simple formation process and less legal formalities. MCA has introduced another GPR by introducing web service RUN-LLP (Reserve Unique Name – Limited Liability Partnership) (replacing Form 1 for reservation/change of name) for the reservation of the name and an integrated form FiLLiP (Form for incorporation of Limited Liability Partnership) (replacing Form 2), and the process has been centralized making incorporation of LLP a complete online process. FiLLiP is the single integrated application for name reservation, incorporation, and/or application for DIN/DPIN (Designated Partners’ Identification Number) [22].

With these reforms and technological advancements being introduced, entrepreneurs will find ease in starting businesses in India as a part of ease of doing business.

7 India’s commitment to business and technological reforms at the state level through Business Reform Action Plan

To bring competitiveness, efficiency along with transparency in the regulatory system, over the last many years, the Indian government has been taking actions to improve upon the regulatory climate for business operations. To provide right momentum and impetus toward the implementation of business reforms across various states and union territories, steps toward the comprehensive efforts and partnership between the central and state governments have been initiated which have facilitated toward bringing conducive and encouraging business regulatory environment for the businesses for promoting industrial growth and thereby creating better entrepreneurship opportunities.

State governments’ role in the countrywide business reform exercise boosts the investors’ confidence. On the similar lines as the World Bank’s Doing Business EODB indicator’s score and assessment, DIPP’s initiative is to spread reforms beyond Delhi and Mumbai (the cities considered for doing business assessment). There was a need to broaden the base from these two cities as scored by the World Bank. The Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade (DPIIT), the Ministry of Commerce and Industry, Government of India, introduced the BRAP. Ranking of states based on the implementation of BRAP started in the year 2014. Under BRAP, all the states and union territories of India are ranked pan India dependent on the business reforms undertaken on the basis of a set of chosen parameters on the similar lines of the Doing Business Report. India is among few nations having state-specific rankings, thereby helping the nation to improve its ranking. This ranking is done with the purpose of creating enabling and conducive regulatory business environment by reducing the regulations and thereby grading the states based on various measures of EODB. The DPIIT is responsible for the formulation and application of various measures relating to the development and growth of the industrial sector within the broader economic objectives. The various measures undertaken desires simplifying and digitizing the regulatory procedures/approvals, reducing the physical interaction as much as possible with the government officials, eliminating the discretionary processes, and expediting time-bound processes and other procedures as mentioned in BRAP recommendations. The Ease of Doing Business state rankings reflect the state’s efforts to encourage competition and cut down on transaction and compliance costs. The BRAP has helped the states to enhance speed along with more transparency in the administrative processes and rationalizes the regulatory framework [17].

The DPIIT shares State Reforms Action Plan with States/Union territories to improve EODB. The Action Plans are spread across various reform areas which follow sector-specific approach to ensure coverage of all sectors so as to create enabling business environment across various sectors for setting up and for operating business in the state. Broadly the sector-specific reforms relate to making available information through Standard Operating Procedures to public, bringing policy and procedural modifications relating to amendments to Act and rules through various government orders, circulars, and so on by different departments and online system offering services by different departments with minimal physical interface and integration of services through single window portal. These rankings represent the ease of doing business in the states ranging from showing better transparency, increased efficiency, effective regulatory functions for the business setups focusing on low-cost and quick single desk approvals, the development of online systems, and regulating inspections covering all the aspects of the business [31]. According to the Department of Industrial Policy and Promotion, Government of India, under the State Reform Action Plan, 2019, Andhra Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh, Telangana, Madhya Pradesh, and Jharkhand are the top five states where business can be started and carried on smoothly. While in other states like Arunachal Pradesh, Meghalaya, Sikkim, Tripura, Nagaland, Odisha, and Manipur, entrepreneurs and businesses find difficulty in starting and operating business. As of March 2021, 20 states in India have successfully undertaken all the major business reforms for the ease of doing business, making them qualified for additional borrowing of 0.25 percent of the gross state domestic product. These 20 states have become eligible to raise Rs. 39,521 crores from the open market borrowings, as permitted by the Department of Expenditure. The developments due to the EODB reforms by the states will lead to faster growth of the states and the economy [39].

8 Single e-compliance window

To bring down the duplication of data filing by the businesses, the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA), DPIIT, Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI), and the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) together are considering the proposal to collaborate for developing a single e-compliance window as the online platform without the need for the physical visit to several departments, with respect to application forms which share common data resources. The major goal of the proposed move is integrating the databases of all the ministries with which businesses have to register and reducing the compliance burden. With this platform, all the companies can upload the applications only at one portal and all the regulators shall access the data from that single repository. Single online compliance window system is a key reform point in the state action plan [25]. The features which are envisaged in this system are online submission, payment of application fee, following up of application status, downloading the certificate, and third-party verification. Such single repository can fulfil 80–90% requirements and any other additional requirement can be accessed through additional information requested separately. Registration and renewal of the licenses, approvals of permissions under various acts like the Factories Act 1948, the Boilers Act 1923, the Contracts Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act 1970, the Shop and Establishment Act, the Building and Other Construction Workers (Regulation of Employment and Conditions of Service) Act 1996, the Inter State Migrant Workmen (RE&CS) Act 1979, Consent under the Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981) &Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act 1974, Authorization under the Hazardous and Other Wastes (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules 2016, Registration/Renewal under Plastic Waste (Management and Handling) Rules 2011, the E-waste (Management and Handling) Rules 2011, and so on. The dashboard regularly updates highlighting the number of registrations and renewals and the average time taken in obtaining the necessary approvals.

Various other reform areas or the investment enablers as mentioned in the 301 reforms of BRAP 2020 are as follows:

  • Designing and implementing an online wizard/system where a user can enter in details like industry type, number of employees, location, and so on and can access the information about the approvals applicable for pre-establishment and pre-starting operations for the industrial unit. The online wizard shall also have access to online application forms link for various licenses/NOCs and the notifications. It shall provide the details regarding the fees, time taken, documents required, departments involved for the required NOCs or registrations, and so on, leading to accessibility to information in transparent manner.

  • Legislative enactments ensuring the time bound delivery to the industries within the state, failing which, the violators shall face punitive action.

  • State investment facilitation center or the promotion agency for investment promotion, underlining working procedures, service timelines, publishing online dashboard for grievance handling and updating regularly for the resolutions [31].

9 Conclusion and the challenges ahead

This chapter outlined the change brought about by the information technology revolution and its significance for the business growth and entrepreneurial development through the government encouragement and adaptation in the form of introducing technological-backed reforms. These included amendments and legislative reforms, tax reforms, banking reforms, and relaxed economic and trade policies which help the economy to grow. India is undergoing a period of unparalleled technological and economic advancement. Indian businesses have taken the advantage of the knowledge resources to upgrade their growth chart owing to the technological revolution using human capital facilitating the innovation and entrepreneurship and moving more closer to the international practices.

India is also moving swiftly toward digitization, and more and more government departments are deploying electronic platforms for public–government interactions, filing, and so on. For India, progress on the ease of doing business is a vital achievement as we seek greater global investments for lifting hundreds of millions to improved standards of living. As is observed in the World Bank report, economies that have adopted electronic means of compliance with regulatory requirements – such as obtaining licenses and paying taxes – experience a lower incidence of bribery, regulatory inefficiency, and red tape.

The regulatory landscape regime is continuously evolving. Various initiatives have been undertaken toward “Ease of Starting Business” by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs over the years for benefiting a large number of stakeholders. Latest web technologies have provided a variety of applications and digital tools on web for the business organizations, one of which is the SPICe+ web form for ease of incorporating companies. The creation of this form for the company registration through the agile and robust web platform of information will lead to smarter user experiences saving on time and efforts. Various emerging technologies like artificial intelligence, internet of things, machine learning, and 5G will further enhance the ease of doing business.

Harmonization of regulations with the global benchmarks results in reduction of regulatory procedures and thereby reduction in the compliance requirements. Rules and regulatory processes and their enforcement should be simple, prompt, effective, real, friendly, effective as well as emphatic to make India competitive in the global regulatory setting. The support from technological advancements will increase the swiftness of ease of incorporation and will further persuade the law makers and law enforcement agencies to bring changes and at the same time address to the challenges thrown by the adoption of technology and artificial intelligence at the workplace. For greater ease of doing business, consideration also has to be given for the impact of advancing technologies as well as the challenges and options that such technologies will present to the corporate, business, and legal ecosystem [25].

Various procedures that used to take many days can now be completed easily with the help of IT and technology solutions. With the Internet of Things (IoT), Web 3.0, artificial intelligence, machine-to-machine learning, and 5G several procedures will get easily connected and completed over the Internet; enabling and letting millions of applications to be handled and processed with the help of devices that can configure themselves, gather and process data with the autonomous devices, monitor usage, self-diagnose, and self-rectify faults, among other things. The Industrial IoT has opened various new possibilities for communications based upon intelligent and self-directed devices and bringing in industrial revolution entirely based on data [13].

The growth and development boosters in Indian economy like beneficial demographic conditions, talented human capital, strong start-up culture, increasing productivity, supportive government policy initiatives and a preferred investment destination for the foreign investors has helped to build a positive growth momentum. The policy initiatives are providing motivation and impetus to enable a friendly business environment and can make a visible difference in many critical areas too. The government has remained focused on enhancing the situation of the manufacturing sector in the country through its ‘Make in India’ project launched in the year 2014 as a program aiming to transform India into a global center for manufacturing and becoming important part of the global supply chain. All these efforts will make doing business simpler for start-ups in India and will establish an ecosystem to boost growth of start-ups and also create an enabling framework for receiving foreign investment, simplifying and cutting out the tedious paperwork/documentation and reporting procedures making India more investor friendly.

Information Technology and ease of doing business leads to transparent business processes and increased business growth. But adoption of technology may raise various issues regarding real-time performance, information privacy, security challenges that arise due to sensitive and confidential information, internal security, health hazards, environmental sustainability, which are also to be considered. Also, there are still many areas where there is immense scope for further improvement. Indeed, great progress has been achieved and but still much more has to be done. Despite the government’s push, it is the receptiveness and efficiency of the administrative system that shall hold the key to the success. Technology can lead to growth and improvement in investment atmosphere only when the benefits accruing from technology are properly integrated into the conventional system of effective economic governance with the help of knowledge products and resources.

A Study on Currently Practiced Effective Training Methods from a Knowledge Worker’s Perspective in Outsourcing Services Sector

Tarakeshwar Gupta
Anuradha R. Tiwari

Abstract

This book chapter delves into the various training methods used in practice to address the various training objectives or training needs identified in the outsourcing services sector comprising BPO and KPO (business process outsourcing and knowledge process outsourcing). The employees who conduct or are associated to conducting these training programs are knowledge workers (KWs) such as trainers, learning, and development professionals, and managers. These KWs play a crucial role in their respective organizations in the outsourcing services sector and often help, train, equip, mentor, employees who lack skills required to execute a certain task. KWs in outsourcing services sector play the most crucial roles as they often have to implement policies and act as a critical communication point between the senior and junior managers. However, this group is often neglected when it comes to their own development. Middle managers often lack planning/project/financial management skills which are very critical to implementation of policy. Many a times, they have the knowhow of the job (the technical aspects of it) but are woefully short of people management skills, problem solving skills, interpersonal skills, and so on, which eventually are a large part of their KRAs (key responsibility area) now. Hence, it becomes imperative to equip them with pertinent skills. Middle management or managers are a part of KWs and hence training these knowledge workers with required skill becomes very important. Training methodology includes methods, materials, techniques, and resources used to implement the training or workshop and transfer new knowledge, skills, and attitudes to participants. The primary objective of this book chapter is to explore the latest trends in training methods in the outsourcing services sector from a KW’s perspective in India. The chapter will also explore the effectiveness of these training methods in terms of achieving their training goals/training objectives with respect to training needs. These training needs are identified through a questionnaire survey from a KW’s perspective. Some of the training objectives identified in outsourcing services sector are acquiring new knowledge or skill, change in attitude, problem-solving skills, enhancement of interpersonal skills, and retention of knowledge and to address these training needs the training methods are lecture method, programmed instruction method, case study method, sensitivity training method, role-playing method, business games method, movie films method, conference (discussion) method, and television lecture method. For this exploratory study, data was collected in the form of questionnaire from knowledge workers who are responsible for capability building or boosting intellectual capital in the outsourcing services sector. Questionnaires emailed to KWs and were 76; filled were 60, which is a return rate of 78.94%. Snowball technique has been used to collect samples. The findings illustrate extensively used effective training methods such as role-playing training method followed by programmed instruction method, and case study training method. Since Web 3.0 has come up as a technically advanced medium that allows users to read, write, and execute and furthermore it enables machines to perform which earlier was expected from humans only; hence, it has the potential to play a huge role in the future to be used a technology in training of knowledge workers. The chapter also focuses on usage of Web 3.0 in training of knowledge workers in the outsourcing services sector which can further enhance capability building or boosting intellectual capital.

Keywords: Training Methods, Effective Web 3.0, ITeS, Outsourcing, Knowledge Worker (KWs),

1 Introduction

Knowledge Workers (KWs) in the outsourcing services sector such as trainers, learning and development professionals, and managers play crucial roles. These KWs also help in upskilling employees who lack skills needed to perform a certain task. The KWs also have to frequently enforce policies and serve as the key point of contact between senior and junior managers. However, this group is often overlooked when it comes to their own development. Middle managers also lack skills in planning/project/financial management skills that are very important to policy implementation. They often have the knowledge of the job (the technical aspects of it) but are utterly devoid skills pertaining to managing people, problem-solving abilities, and interpersonal skills, and so on, which are now a large part of their KRAs (key responsibility areas). Therefore, equipping them appropriate skills is crucial. Middle management or managers are essentially a part of KWs.

Training is an important element in creating a high-work performance culture [1]. Training is used to enhance knowledge, skill, and attitude deficiencies. According to Mandakini [2], training methodology includes methods, materials, techniques, and resources used to implement the training or workshop and transfer new knowledge, skills, and attitudes to participants. Before selecting the training methodology, training objectives or training need identification (TNI) needs to be done to address the knowledge, skill, and attitude deficiencies.

2 Research objectives

The primary objective of this research-based book chapter is to explore the latest trends in training methods in the outsourcing services sector from a KWs perspective in India. The book chapter will also explore the effectiveness of these training methods in terms of achieving their training goals/training objectives with respect to training needs. These training needs are identified through a questionnaire survey from a KW’s perspective. Also, it will explore the usage of Web 3.0 in training of knowledge workers.

3 Literature review

Most companies decide to outsource their operational activities such as customer services, HR, payment services, insurance claims, administration, and so on to another organization who in turn have developed core competency in these areas; such organizations are outsourcing services companies. Individuals working in such organizations are KWs as they exhibit a certain amount of knowledge to execute their tasks.

The literature review summary is shown in Table 1.

Table 1:Literature review summary table.

Terms Sources
Acquiring new knowledge or skill Skinner [12, 13, 14]
Adult learning Maria & Elena [5]
